## 1. Background information on the procedure

Pursuant to Article 16 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 1234/2008, Moderna Biotech Spain S.L. submitted to the European Medicines Agency on 06 March 2026 an application for a variation.

The following changes were proposed:

| Variation(s) requested   | Variation(s) requested                                                                                                                                     | Type              |
|--------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.4                      | C.4 Change(s) in the summary of product characteristics, labelling or package leaflet due to new quality, preclinical, clinical or pharmacovigilance data. | Variation type II |

Update of sections 4.2, 4.8 and 5.1 of the SmPC to update the posology recommendation for the 2 years through 4 years age group, based on final results from the study mRNA-1273-P306 listed as a category 3 study in the RMP; this is an Open-Label, Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Safety and Immunogenicity of the mRNA Vaccines for SARS-CoV-2 Variants in Participants Aged 6 Months to &lt;6 Years; the Package Leaflet is updated accordingly. The RMP version 14.0 has also been submitted. In addition, the MAH took the opportunity to update the list of local representatives in the Package Leaflet.

The requested variation(s) proposed amendments to the Summary of Product Characteristics and Package Leaflet and to the Risk Management Plan (RMP).

## 2. Overall conclusion and impact on the benefit/risk balance

The purpose of study mRNA-1273-P306 was to evaluate the immune response against SARS-CoV-2 variants of concern (VOC) after primary-series vaccination or booster dose of variant adapted vaccines in children 6 months through 5 years of age.

In part 1 the mRNA-1273.214 primary series induced an increase in nAbs regardless of SARS-CoV-2 infection status at Baseline. The pre-specified success criteria for the primary immunogenicity objective were met, thus establishing immunobridging to Study P204 and thereby inferring vaccine effectiveness.

In part 2 the primary immunogenicity analyses demonstrated that the immune response against Omicron BA.1 elicited by the mRNA-1273.214 BD (10µg) in Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age was superior, and the immune response against D614G was non-inferior, compared to the immune response elicited by the mRNA-1273 2 dose primary series in Study P204 participants of the same age range. The primary immunogenicity analysis was based on the PPIS-Neg.

In part 3 the mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 µg) elicited a substantial increase in antibody levels against Omicron XBB.1.5 in previously vaccinated participants aged 6 months through 5 years. The analysis was based on the PPIS.

While immunogenicity analyses from parts 1 through 3 are reassuring the use of variant-adapted mRNA1273 vaccines as a 2-dose primary series regimen in SARS-CoV-2 naïve participants and a single dose after SARS-CoV-2 primary-series vaccination, results from part 4 demonstrated that a single 25 µg dose of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort A PPIS-Pos elicited a non-inferior immune response against XBB.1.5 compared to a 25 µg 2-dose primary series of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort B PPIS-Neg. What has not been demonstrated with the submitted results is the comparison of a single dose in participants 2 - 4 years of age that have not been vaccinated previously and no history of SARS-CoV-2 infection versus 2-dose primary series vaccination. The MAH argues that sero-epidemiology has changed and individuals in this age group can be considered seropositive for SARS-CoV-2. This was requested to be further substantiated, and/or non-inferiority of a single dose in 2 - 4 year old SARS-CoV-2 naïve children without

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination versus a 2-dose primary series should be demonstrated. The MAH agreed in its response that the submitted data do not demonstrate non-inferiority of a single dose compared with a 2 dose primary series in SARS-CoV-2-naïve children aged 2 to &lt;5 years. The MAH further responded that it will no longer pursue the proposed single-dose posology for children aged 2 to &lt;5 years and proposes to maintain the currently approved 2-dose primary series for all children in this age group, which is accepted.

Overall, solicited and unsolicited AEs were consistent with the known safety profile of Spikevax, and no new safety concerns were identified.

The RMP is updated to version 14.1 in order to remove long-term safety as missing information following the completion of Study mRNA 1273-P306, which fulfils the long-term safety commitment, and additionally based on the extensive safety information for Spikevax and its variant-containing vaccines; and remove the study mRNA 1273-P306 from Parts III.2 and III.3 following its completion. Only a single category 3 additional pharmacovigilance activity remains ongoing. The MAH introduced other updates to the RMP.

The MAH submitted a variation application under category C.4 of the variation classification Guideline with the scope to include \"Update of sections 4.2, 4.8 and 5.1 of the SmPC to update the posology recommendation for the 2 years through 4 years age group, based on final results from the study mRNA1273-P306 listed as a category 3 study in the RMP; this is an Open-Label, Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Safety and Immunogenicity of the mRNA Vaccines for SARS-CoV-2 Variants in Participants Aged 6 Months to &lt;6 Years; the Package Leaflet is updated accordingly. The RMP version 14.0 has also been submitted. In addition, the MAH took the opportunity to update the list of local representatives in the Package Leaflet\". Based on the assessment of the data contained in the application, the CHMP is of the view that the following changes to the MA should be introduced \"Submission of final results from study mRNA1273-P306 listed as a category 3 study in the RMP; this is an open-label, phase 3 study to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of the mRNA vaccines for SARS-CoV-2 variants in participants aged 6 months to &lt;6 years. The risk management plan is updated to version 14.1.\" These changes fall under category C.12 of the variation classification Guideline.

In reply to the Request for Supplementary Information, the MAH updated the electronic Application Form accordingly.

The benefit-risk balance of Spikevax remains positive.

## 3. Recommendations

Based on the review of the submitted data, this application regarding the following change:

| Variation(s) requested   | Variation(s) requested                                                                                                                                                       | Type              |
|--------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.12                     | C.12 Other variations not specifically covered elsewhere in this Annex which involve the submission of studies, including bioequivalence studies, to the competent authority | Variation type II |

Submission of final results from study mRNA-1273-P306 listed as a category 3 study in the RMP; this is an open-label, phase 3 study to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of the mRNA vaccines for SARSCoV-2 variants in participants aged 6 months to &lt;6 years. The risk management plan is updated to version 14.1.

is recommended for approval.

## Annex: Rapporteur's assessment comments on the type II variation

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

## 5. Introduction

Currently, in the EU and UK, children 6 months through 4 years of age who are previously unvaccinated for COVID-19 and have no known history of SARS-CoV-2 infection receive 2 doses of Spikevax administered 28 days apart. This regimen was initially authorized in the EU with EMA procedure EMEA/H/C/005791/II/0067 that approved the use of mRNA-1273 25 µg for active immunisation to prevent COVID-19 caused by SARS-CoV-2 in individuals 6 months to &lt;6 years of age administered as a primary series of 2 doses (0.25 mL each) 28 days apart. This authorisation was supported by results from Study P204, in which safety and efficacy were established in a predominantly infection-naïve population.

However, during procedure EMEA/H/C/005791/II/0104/G, Spikevax was approved as a single dose vaccine for individuals 5 years of age and older, regardless of vaccination history. The rationale for simplification was based on changing epidemiology with most of the population developing pre-existing immunity through immunisation and/or infection. Since then, with SARS-CoV-2 becoming endemic, the MAH claims that the vast majority of children 2 years through 4 years of age are also seropositive, and prevalence rates are similar to those in individuals 5 years and older. Accordingly, a single dose regimen (as currently authorized for individuals 5 years of age and older, with or without prior vaccination; EMEA/H/C/005791/II/0104/G) is claimed to be appropriate for children 2 years through 4 years of age.

Data from Study P306 Part 4 are provided to demonstrate effectiveness of a single-dose regimen in predominantly seropositive children 2 years through 4 years of age. These data are meant to support a change in dosing posology for 2 years through 4 years old previously unvaccinated individuals. Accordingly, with this submission the expansion of the age range for the single dose regimen down to 2 years of age is claimed.

This submission provides immunogenicity data for mRNA-1273 formulations from the Study mRNA-1273P306, hereafter referred to as Study P306. Study P306 was an open-label, Phase 3 study to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of mRNA-1273 variant-containing formulations (mRNA-1273.214 and mRNA1273.815) in individuals 6 months through 5 years of age. The study was initiated in Jun 2022, initially consisting of only Part 1 and Part 2 that were completed in Jun 2024. Part 3 and Part 4 were added in Nov 2023 in response to evolving SARS-CoV-2 epidemiology impacting pediatric dosing regimen and were completed in Oct 2025. Each part evaluated the epidemiologically relevant vaccine formulation at the time, as below:

- Part 1 evaluated mRNA-1273.214 administered as a 2-dose primary series (25 µg/dose; 2 doses, 28 days apart) in participants 6 months through 5 years of age not previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2.
- Part 2 evaluated mRNA-1273.214 administered as a single 10-µg BD in participants 6 months through 5 years of age who previously received mRNA-1273 25 µg as a 2-dose primary series.
- Part 3 evaluated mRNA-1273.815 administered as a single 25-µg BD in participants 6 months through 5 years of age previously vaccinated with an authorized/approved COVID-19 vaccine primary series regimen.
- Part 4 evaluated mRNA-1273.815 administered as a single 25-µg dose in participants 2 years through 4 years of age not previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2 (Part 4A; treatment group). The immune response in Part 4A was compared to that after mRNA-1273.815 administered as a 2 dose primary series (25 µg/dose; 28 days apart) in participants 6 months through 23 months of age not previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2 (Part 4B; active control group).

## 6. Clinical Efficacy aspects

Key study design details for all 4 parts from study P306 are summarised below.

The study population, design, dose schedule, number of participants exposed, and primary and secondary immunogenicity objectives and endpoints for Study P306 are provided in Efficacy Table 1.

Table 1:Design of Completed Study mRNA-1273-P306

| Study Number (Countries)    | Study Design                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Study Populations                                                   | Regimen (Participants in the Safety Set)                   | Primary and Secondary Immunogenicity Objectives and Endpoints                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Abbreviations: COVID-19 = coronavirus disease 2019; D = day; m = months; S-2P = spike protein modified with 2 proline substitutions within the heptad repeat 1 domain; SARS-CoV-2 = severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2; US = United States; VOC = variant of concern; y = years.

- a. mRNA-1273.214 contains 2 mRNAs (1:1 ratio), one that encodes S-2P of the Wuhan-Hu-1 isolate of SARS-CoV-2 and the other that encodes S-2P of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron variant (B.1.1.529 subvariant BA.1).
- b. \"Study P204\" refers to Study mRNA-1273-P204, a Phase 2/3, dose-escalation, age de-escalation (Part 1, open-label) and randomized, observer-blind, placebo-controlled expansion study (Part 2) to evaluate the safety, reactogenicity, and effectiveness of mRNA-1273 (primary series and BD) and safety of mRNA-1273.214 (BD) in children 6 months through 11 years of age, assessing up to 3 dose levels (25 µg, 50 µg, and 100 µg) of mRNA-1273 in the primary series.
- c. Sero-response at participant level was defined as an Ab value change from Baseline (pre-Dose 1 of primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline was ≥ the LLOQ.
- d. Sero-response at participant level was defined as an Ab value change from Baseline (pre-Dose 1 of primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline was ≥ the LLOQ and &lt;4 × LLOQ, or at least a 2-fold rise if Baseline was ≥4 × LLOQ.
- e. Sero-response at participant level was defined as an Ab value change from pre-BD below the LLOQ to post-BD ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if pre-BD was ≥ the LLOQ.
- f. Sero-response at participant level was defined as an Ab value change from pre-BD below the LLOQ to post-BD ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if pre-BD was ≥ the LLOQ and ≤4 × LLOQ, or at least a 2-fold rise if pre-BD was ≥4 × LLOQ.
- g. mRNA-1273.815 contains a single mRNA that encodes S-2P of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron subvariant XBB.1.5.
- h. Sero-response at participant level was defined as the antibody value changed from Baseline (prior to first dose of the 2-dose series or prior to the single dose) below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline was ≥LLOQ.

Source: Study P306 Final CSR Part 1 and Part 2, Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, and Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A.

## 6.1. Methods - analysis of data submitted

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Part 1 enrolled participants 6 months through 5 years of age who had not been previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2 (this included participants from this age group who received placebo in Study P204). Participants received mRNA-1273.214 vaccine administered as a 2 dose primary series (25 µg/dose; 2 doses, 28 days apart). Participants were followed for approximately 12 months after Dose 2.

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of mRNA-1273.214 25 µg 2 dose primary series in participants 6 months through 5 years of age. The basis for demonstrating vaccine effectiveness was measurement of the serum nAb responses. Effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 primary series was inferred by establishing non-inferiority of the immune response against ancestral SARS-CoV-2 and superiority against Omicron BA.1 obtained 28 days post Dose 2 compared to that observed with the mRNA-1273 primary series 28 days post Dose 2 in the corresponding age group in Study P204.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

In Part 2, mRNA-1273.214 was given as a BD to participants who had been previously enrolled in Study P204 and were vaccinated with the mRNA-1273 2 dose primary series. These participants received a 10 µg BD of mRNA-1273.214, at least 4 months after completion of the mRNA-1273 primary series in Study

P204.

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of mRNA-1273.214 10 µg BD in participants 6 months through 5 years of age. The basis for demonstrating vaccine effectiveness was the measurement of the serum nAb responses. Effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 10 µg BD was inferred by establishing non-inferiority of the immune response against ancestral SARS-CoV-2 and superiority against Omicron BA.1 obtained 28 days post BD compared to that observed with the mRNA-1273 primary series 28 days post Dose 2 in the corresponding age group in Study P204.

Additional details regarding the study design for Study P306 Part 2 are provided in the Study P306 Final CSR Part 1 and Part 2, Section 3.1.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Participants in Part 3 were previously vaccinated with an age-appropriate COVID-19 vaccination regimen of an authorized/approved COVID-19 vaccine, including participants who received mRNA-1273.214 in Parts 1 or 2 of Study P306. Participants received a single 25 µg BD of the mRNA-1273.815 vaccine on Day 1, at least 4 months after receipt of their last COVID-19 vaccine.

There was no primary immunogenicity objective. The secondary immunogenicity objective was to evaluate the immune responses against Omicron XBB.1.5 induced by mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 µg) as measured by the variant-specific serum Ab GM value and SRR 28 days after dosing (ie, BD-Day 29). To contextualize the immunogenicity of a single dose in previously vaccinated individuals with those in vaccine-naïve individuals, immunogenicity endpoints were compared to Part 4 immunogenicity results.

Additional details regarding the study design for Study P306 Part 3 are provided in the Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Section 9.1.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 years) and 2-dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

Participants in Part 4 were all vaccine-naïve. Cohort A received a single 25 µg dose of the mRNA1273.815 vaccine on Day 1. Participants in Part 4 Cohort B received 2 doses of 25 µg of the mRNA1273.815 vaccine, 28 days apart (i.e., on Day 1 and Day 29).

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of a single 25 µg dose of mRNA1273.815 in participants aged 2 years through 4 years. The basis for demonstrating vaccine effectiveness was the measurement of the serum nAb responses. Effectiveness of the single 25 µg dose of the mRNA1273.815 vaccine was inferred by establishing non-inferiority of the immune response in participants aged 2 through 4 years (Cohort A) who had prior SARS-CoV-2 infection relative to 25 μg mRNA-1273.815 given as the 2-dose primary series in participants aged 6 months through 23 months (Cohort B) who did not have prior SARS-CoV-2 infection.

Additional details regarding the study design for Study P306 Part 4 are provided in the Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A, Section 3.1.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Assays Used for Immunogenicity Analyses

A summary of the validated analytical methods used for the assessment of clinical endpoints discussed in this document is provided in Efficacy Table 2.

Table 2: Overview of Bioassays for the Assessment of Clinical Endpoints in Study P306

| Assay Name                             | Variant               | Methodology                          | Study Numbers      | Development Status (Performing Laboratory)   |
|----------------------------------------|-----------------------|--------------------------------------|--------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| SARS-CoV-2 PsV neutralisation (VAC62)  | Ancestral (D614G) a,b | PsV neutralisation measured as AU/mL | P306 Parts 1 and 2 | Validated (PPD vaccine laboratories)         |
| SARS-CoV-2 PsV neutralisation (VAC122) | Omicron BA.1          | PsV neutralisation measured as AU/mL | P306 Parts 1 and 2 | Validated (PPD vaccine laboratories)         |
| SARS-CoV-2 PsV neutralisation (VAC150) | XBB.1.5               | PsV neutralisation measured as AU/mL | P306 Parts 3 and 4 | Validated (PPD vaccine laboratories)         |

Abbreviations: AU = arbitrary units; nAb = neutralising antibody; PPD = Pharmaceutical Product Development; PsV = pseudovirus; PsVN = pseudovirus neutralisation; SARS-CoV-2 = severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2.

a nAb response against D614G was assessed in Study P204 participants who received the mRNA-1273 25 µg 2-dose primary series. This assessment was part of immunogenicity endpoints for Study P306 Parts 1 (Section 1.1.1), 2 (Section 1.1.2), and 4 (Section 1.1.4).

b Neutralisation of the ancestral SARS-CoV-2 strain was assessed in a PsVN assay, where the Wuhan-Hu-1 spike protein used in the assay also included a D614G mutation, a mutation that appeared in the virus early in 2020. The ancestral SARS-CoV-2 strain neutralisation results are therefore designed as D614G.

## Analysis Populations

Analysis populations are defined in Efficacy Table 3.

Table 3: Analysis Populations

| Analysis Population   | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|-----------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Immunogenicity Set    | A set of participants in the FAS who provided immunogenicity samples and had at least 1 postbaseline antibody assessment, regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| PPIS                  | All participants in the Immunogenicity Set who received planned doses of the study vaccine per schedule, complied with immunogenicity testing schedule, had no major protocol deviations that impacted key or critical data, were not receiving HAART if diagnosed with HIV, had antibody assessments on Day 29 (Part 4A), or Day 57 (Part 1 and Part 4B), or BD-Day 29 (Part 2 and Part 3) for the analysis endpoint, and had a Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status available. |
| PPIS-Neg              | All participants in the PPIS who were SARS-CoV-2 negative (no serologic or virologic evidence of prior SARS-CoV-2 infection) before receiving the study vaccine.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| PPIS-Pos              | All participants in the PPIS who were SARS-CoV-2 positive (serologic or virologic evidence of prior SARS-CoV-2 infection) before receiving the study vaccine.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

Abbreviations: BD = booster dose; COVID-19 = coronavirus disease 2019; FAS = Full Analysis Set; HAART = highly active antiretroviral therapy; HIV = human immunodeficiency virus; PPIS = Per-protocol Immunogenicity Set; PPIS-Neg = Per-protocol Immunogenicity Set-Negative; PPIS-Pos = Per-protocol Immunogenicity Set-Positive; SARS-CoV-2 = severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2.

Note: Participants were analysed according to the treatment actually received.

## Analysis Methods

Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 25 µg 2 dose primary series in participants 6 months through 5 years of age by comparing induced serum nAb responses with those obtained from participants in the same age group after the original mRNA-1273 primary series in Study P204. The nAb response was measured 28 days after Dose 2 (Day 57).

Analyses were performed based on participants in the PPIS (see Efficacy Table 3 for definition). Given the increase in seroprevalence in the general population at the time of study conduct, the PPIS included all participants regardless of whether they were Baseline SARS-CoV-2 negative or positive. Accordingly, the PPIS was defined in the same manner for the historical comparator (Study P204).

The primary immunogenicity objective was met if the success criteria for the coprimary endpoints were demonstrated as follows:

- For GMR (Study P306 Part 1 versus Study P204):
- Superiority of the GMR of nAb against Omicron BA.1 using a margin of 1.0 (i.e., lower bound of the 95% CI of the GMR &gt;1.0).
- Non-inferiority of the GMR of nAb against D614G using a margin of 1.5 (i.e., lower bound of the 95% CI of the GMR &gt;0.667).

SRRs against Omicron BA.1 and D614G in Study P306 Part 1 were compared with those in Study P204 as secondary objectives. The SRR difference between Study P306 Part 1 participants at Day 57 and Study P204 participants 6 months through 5 years of age at Day 57 was provided. Sero-response at the participant level was defined in 2 ways as follows:

- Definition 1: Antibody value change from Baseline (pre-dose 1 of primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4 fold rise if Baseline was ≥LLOQ.
- Definition 2: Antibody value change from Baseline (pre-dose 1 of primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4 fold rise if Baseline was ≥LLOQ and &lt;4×LLOQ, or at least a 2 fold rise if Baseline was ≥4×LLOQ.

Incidence rates of COVID-19 and SARS-CoV-2 infection and persistence of the immune responses of mRNA-1273.214 primary series were assessed as an additional exploratory endpoint.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 10 µg BD in Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age by comparing induced serum nAb levels and SRR at BD Day 29 (28 days after the BD) with post primary series (Day 57; 28 days after Dose 2) in Study P204 participants 6 months through 5 years of age. Analyses were performed based on participants in the PPIS-Neg; see Efficacy Table 3 for definition.

The primary immunogenicity objective was met if the success criteria for the coprimary endpoints were demonstrated as follows:

- For GMR (Study P306 Part 2 versus Study P204):
- Superiority of the nAb response against Omicron BA.1 using a margin of 1.0 (ie, a lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;1.0).
- Non-inferiority of the nAb response against D614G using a margin of 1.5 (ie, a lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;0.667).

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- For SRR difference (Study P306 Part 2 versus Study P204):
- Non-inferiority of the nAb sero-response against Omicron BA.1 using a margin of 5% (ie, a lower bound of the 95% CI &gt; -5%).
- Non-inferiority of the nAb sero-response against D614G using a margin of 10% (ie, a lower bound of the 95% CI &gt; -10%).

Sero-response at a participant level was defined as a change from Baseline (pre-dose 1 of the prior primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4 fold rise if Baseline was ≥LLOQ.

Persistence of immune response following the BD through BD Day 181 and incidence rates of COVID-19 and SARS-CoV-2 infection were assessed as exploratory endpoints for the BD analysis.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

No primary immunogenicity analyses were planned for Study P306 Part 3.

The secondary immunogenicity objective was to evaluate the immune responses against Omicron XBB.1.5 induced by a single mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 µg) as measured by the variant specific serum Ab GM value and SRR 28 days after dosing (ie, BD-Day 29).

Analyses were performed based on participants in the PPIS (see Efficacy Table 3 for definition). The results of these assessments are presented in Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Section 11.1.

For full details of the statistical analyses carried out for immunogenicity assessments in Study P306 Part 3, refer to the Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Section 9.7.1.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 years) and 2-dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

The primary immunogenicity objective was to infer the effectiveness of a single 25 μg dose of mRNA1273.815 in vaccine-naïve participants aged 2 years through 4 years. Immune response (serum antibody GM value) against Omicron XBB.1.5 was assessed 28 days postdose (Day 29) in participants aged 2 years through 4 years (Cohort A). For the active control group, immune response was assessed on Day 57 (28 days after the second dose) following two 25 μg doses of mRNA-1273.815 in vaccine-naïve participants aged 6 months through 23 months (Cohort B).

Analyses were performed based on participants in Cohort A PPIS-Pos versus Cohort B PPIS-Neg (see Efficacy Table 3 for definition). The primary immunogenicity objective was met if the success criteria for the primary endpoint were demonstrated as follows:

- GMR (Cohort A versus Cohort B): Non-inferiority of the GMR of nAb against Omicron XBB.1.5 using a margin of 1.5 (ie, lower bound of the 95% CI of the GMR &gt;0.667).

The secondary immunogenicity objectives were to assess the immunogenicity of a single 25 μg dose of mRNA-1273.815 on Day 29, starting with comparison of sero-response against Omicron XBB.1.5 in Cohort A PPIS-Pos to that on Day 57 in Cohort B PPIS-Neg after two 25 μg doses of mRNA-1273.815. The non-inferiority of SRR was to be considered achieved if the lower bound of the 95% CI of the SRR difference (Cohort A PPIS-Pos Day 29-Cohort B PPIS-Neg Day 57) was &gt;-10%.

The exploratory immunogenicity endpoints assessed immune responses against Omicron XBB.1.5 on Day 29 in Part 4A, measured by SRR using definition 'b' that was similar to definition 'a' except for

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

participants with Baseline nAb levels ≥LLOQ (at least 2-fold rise versus at least 4-fold rise, defined below). The results of these assessments are presented in the study CSR (Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A, Section 6).

- Definition 'a': Sero-response at participant level is defined as an Ab value change from Baseline (pre-Dose 1 of primary series, or prior to single dose) below the LLOQ to ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline is ≥ LLOQ
- Definition 'b': Sero-response at participant level is defined as an Ab value change from Baseline (pre-Dose 1 of primary series, or prior to single dose) below the LLOQ to ≥4 × LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline is ≥ LLOQ and &lt;4 × LLOQ, or at least a 2-fold rise if Baseline is ≥4 × LLOQ.

For full details of the statistical analyses carried out for immunogenicity assessments in Study P306 Part 1, refer to the Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A, Section 4.1.

## 6.2. Results

Overall, the results from Study P306 support the use of mRNA-1273 formulations in children. These results build upon the previously demonstrated efficacy of mRNA-1273 in Study P204 (EMEA/H/C/005791/II/0149 submitted to EMA on 22 Nov 2024 and approved on 06 Feb 2025). For children 6 months through 23 months of age who have not previously received a COVID-19 vaccine, a 2dose primary series of an epidemiologically relevant vaccine formulation is expected to generate an adequate immune response. For children 6 months through 23 months of age who completed a primary series previously, the administration of a single dose of an updated formulation restores immune response to the matching variant. For older children, i.e., &gt;2 years of age, the data are intended to support a single dose of the current formulation to be sufficiently immunogenic in the setting of preexisting immunity from prior infection and/or vaccination.

The demographics and Baseline characteristics of the study population are presented in Module 2.5, Section 4.1.2.

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

The mRNA-1273.214 primary series induced an increase in nAbs regardless of SARS-CoV-2 infection status at Baseline. The pre-specified success criteria for the primary immunogenicity objective were met, thus establishing immunobridging to Study P204 and thereby inferring vaccine effectiveness.

The primary analysis to infer vaccine effectiveness was performed in the PPIS; see Efficacy Table 3 for definition. Subgroup analyses in participants with (PPIS-Pos) and without (PPIS-Neg) prior SARS-CoV-2 infection showed that the mRNA-1273.214 2 dose primary series induced a robust rise in nAbs compared to Baseline, regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status.

Results of the secondary immunogenicity objective based on SRR showed that the majority of participants achieved sero-response after the mRNA-1273.214 2 dose primary series, regardless of Baseline SARSCoV-2 status.

## Coprimary Endpoints: Immunogenicity Analyses to Infer Vaccine Effectiveness

The coprimary immunogenicity endpoints to infer effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 2 dose primary series were based on the GMR.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In this section, the results are summarised first for responses measured by PsVNA against Omicron BA.1 and then against D614G.

## Omicron BA.1

Overall, the GMR of Study P306 Part 1 participants 6 months through 5 years of age to Study P204 participants of the same age group for Day 57 Omicron BA.1 nAb levels was 50.829 (95% CI: 44.884, 57.562) (Table 4). Thus, the superiority criterion for GMR was met (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;1.0).

Results of this analysis were consistent with the results of a prior interim analysis (05 Dec 2022 data cutoff) that demonstrated a GMR of 25.4 (95% CI: 20.1, 32.1).

## D614G

Overall, the GMR of Study P306 Part 1 participants 6 months through 5 years of age to Study P204 participants of the same age group for Day 57 D614G nAb levels was 1.194 (95% CI: 1.048, 1.360) (Efficacy Table 4). Thus, the non-inferiority criterion for GMR was met (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;0.667).

Results of this analysis were consistent with the results of a prior interim analysis (05 Dec 2022 data cutoff) that demonstrated a GMR of 0.8 (95% CI: 0.7, 1.0).

## Subgroup Analyses

The primary immunogenicity endpoints were also examined by the following subgroups:

- Age subgroups (2 years through 5 years, 6 months through 23 months).
- Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status (negative, positive).
- Others (sex [male, female], race [White, Black, others], ethnicity [Hispanic or Latino, Not Hispanic or Latino, or missing], race and ethnicity [White non-Hispanic or missing, Communities of Color], obesity, and obesity subgroup [non-obesity or missing]).

## Primary Immunogenicity Analysis by Age Subgroups

The primary immunogenicity analysis was also performed by age subgroups (6 months through 23 months and 2 years through 5 years). In general, results were consistent with those observed for the overall population (6 months through 5 years), except against D614G in the 6 months through 23 months age subgroup, as further described below.

## Omicron BA.1

At Day 57, the nAb levels against Omicron BA.1 were significantly higher in Study P306 Part 1 compared to Study P204 across participants from both age subgroups (2 years through 5 years and 6 months through 23 months). For participants 2 years through 5 years of age, the GMC was 4507.7 (95% CI: 4011.9, 5064.8) in Study P306 Part 1 versus 71.2 (95% CI: 64.8, 78.1) in Study P204. Similarly, for participants 6 months through 23 months of age, the GMC was 2982.0 (95% CI: 2460.9, 3613.4) in Study P306 Part 1 versus 85.6 (95% CI: 76.6, 95.7) in Study P204.

## D614G

The nAb levels against D614G were higher in Study P306 Part 1 compared to Study P204 among participants in the 2 years through 5 years age subgroup, with a GMC of 2781.4 (95% CI: 2449.5, 3158.2) versus 1567.6 (95% CI: 1414.0, 1738.0). However, in the 6 months through 23 months age subgroup, the nAb levels were lower in Study P306 Part 1 compared to Study P204, with a GMC of 1098.6 (95% CI: 913.2, 1321.7) versus 1933.6 (95% CI: 1736.3, 2153.5). Given the extinction of the D614G strain circulation in the general population, the fact that participants in Study P306 Part 1 received half the dose of mRNA-1273 (as contained in mRNA-1273.214) compared to participants in Study P204, and the shift toward monovalent variant containing vaccine formulations that exclude the ancestral SARSCoV-2 strain, the relevance of this finding to future COVID-19 vaccination strategies, which will likely use monovalent vaccines against the most prevalent circulating variant, is unclear.

## Primary Immunogenicity Analysis by Baseline SARS-CoV-2 Status

The primary immunogenicity analysis was also performed for participants with (PPIS-Pos) and without (PPIS-Neg) prior SARS-CoV-2 infection at Baseline. At Baseline, 20.5% of participants were SARS-CoV-2 negative and 75.4% of participants were SARS-CoV-2 positive.

As expected, Baseline nAb levels against Omicron BA.1 and D614G were higher among PPIS-Pos participants than the PPIS-Neg participants. Among the PPIS-Pos participants, Baseline nAb GMC in Study P306 Part 1 as compared to Study P204 were higher against Omicron BA.1 (130.6 [95% CI: 105.4, 161.8] versus 21.0 [95% CI: 16.4, 26.8]) but lower against D614G (74.1 [95% CI: 60.4, 91.0] and

The GMR (P306 Part 1 versus P204) against Omicron BA.1 at Day 57 was 15.098 (95% CI: 12.214, 18.664) and 8.215 (95% CI: 5.737, 11.764) in PPIS-Neg and PPIS-Pos, respectively. Although GMRs (P306 Part 1 versus P204) against D614G at Day 57 were low, 0.321 (95% CI: 0.257, 0.401) and 0.354 (95% CI: 0.239, 0.524) in PPIS-Neg and PPIS-Pos, respectively, the postvaccination results compared to Baseline results with the P306 Part 1 participants show that mRNA-1273.214 primary series induced a robust rise in nAb levels compared to Baseline against both Omicron BA.1 and D614G, regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status (Efficacy Table 5).

## Other Subgroups

No meaningful differences for the immune responses against Omicron BA.1 and against D614G were observed between the following subgroups examined: sex (male, female), race (White, Black, others), ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Not Hispanic or Latino, or missing), race and ethnicity (White non-Hispanic or missing, Communities of Color), obesity, and obesity subgroup (nonobesity or missing).

## Secondary Immunogenicity Results

## Sero-response Rate

SRRs against Omicron BA.1 and D614G in Study P306 Part 1 participants 6 months through 5 years of age were compared with those in Study P204 participants of the same age group as a secondary objective, based on the PPIS. Sero-response at a participant level (for the results presented in this section) was defined as an antibody value change from Baseline (pre-dose 1 of primary series) below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4-fold rise if Baseline was ≥LLOQ.

## Omicron BA.1

Overall, the SRR (Day 57, relative to pre-dose 1 of primary series) against Omicron BA.1 was 95.6% (95% CI: 92.5%, 97.6%) in Study P306 Part 1 and 85.7% (95% CI: 82.7%, 88.4%) in Study P204. The SRR difference (P306 Part 1 versus P204) was 9.8% (95% CI: 6.0%, 13.5%) (Efficacy Table 6).

The analysis of SRRs against Omicron BA.1 by age subgroups (2 years through 5 years and 6 months through 23 months) yielded results consistent with those observed for the overall population.

## D614G

Overall, the SRR (Day 57, relative to pre-dose 1 of primary series) against D614G was 92.9% (95% CI: 89.4%, 95.6%) in Study P306 Part 1 and 99.5% (95% CI: 98.5%, 99.9%) in Study P204. The SRR difference (P306 Part 1 versus P204) was -6.6% (95% CI: -10.1%, -4.0%) (Efficacy Table 6).

When analysed by age subgroups (2 years through 5 years and 6 months through 23 months), the SRR (Day 57, relative to pre-dose 1 of primary series) against D614G was higher among participants 2 years through 5 years of age than participants 6 months through 23 months of age group (98.0% [95% CI: 95.1%, 99.5%] versus 81.7% [95% CI: 72.4%, 89.0%]).

## Analysis of Sero-response Rate by Baseline SARS-CoV-2 Status

In addition to the assessment of immunogenicity, as measured by SRR, in participants regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status (overall [negative or positive]), SRR was also assessed in participants without prior SARS-CoV-2 infection at Baseline (PPIS-Neg) and in participants with prior SARS-CoV-2 infection at Baseline (PPIS-Pos). Results show that regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 infection status, administration of the mRNA-1273.214 2 dose primary series induced measurable increases in nAb levels relative to pre-Dose 1 of primary series levels against both Omicron BA.1 and D614G strains (Efficacy Table 7).

## Exploratory Endpoint - Long term Immunogenicity

The mRNA-1273.214 2 dose primary series demonstrated persistent immunogenicity against both Omicron BA.1 and D614G strains at 6 months (Day 209) after Dose 2. The nAb levels at Day 209 remained elevated compared to Baseline levels against both Omicron BA.1 (1465.5 [95% CI: 1244.2, 1726.2]; n=262) and D614G (564.2 [95% CI: 474.9, 670.2]; n=265). The GMFR was 20.0 (95% CI: 16.2, 24.6) and 9.5 (95% CI: 7.9, 11.3), respectively. The SRRs against Omicron BA.1 and D614G at Day 209 were 82.8% (95% CI: 77.6%, 87.2%; n=212) and 71.8% (95% CI: 65.9%, 77.1%; n=188), respectively.

As a sensitivity analysis for the exploratory immunogenicity analysis, GMC was calculated by excluding participants who had SARS-CoV-2 infection up to the analysis timepoint (Day 209). Results indicate that nAb responses (GMC) against Omicron BA.1 and D614G through 6 months after mRNA-1273.214 primary series waned over time but remained above Baseline levels, regardless of intercurrent SARS-CoV-2 infection.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

The primary immunogenicity analyses demonstrated that the immune response against Omicron BA.1 elicited by the mRNA-1273.214 BD in Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age was superior, and the immune response against D614G was non-inferior, compared to the immune response elicited by the mRNA-1273 2 dose primary series in Study P204 participants of the same age range. The primary immunogenicity analysis was based on the PPIS-Neg.

The immunobridging analysis results demonstrated that original primary series vaccination followed by a variant containing BD, rather than the original primary series vaccination alone, improves immune response against the target variant. These results are consistent with those from the adult variant containing BD studies (Study P205) that also demonstrated immune responses successfully meeting prespecified superiority criteria against the Omicron BA.1 and Omicron BA.4/BA.5 subvariants.

## Coprimary Endpoints: Immunogenicity Analyses to Infer BD Effectiveness

The coprimary immunogenicity endpoints to infer effectiveness of the mRNA-1273.214 BD were based on GMR and SRR.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In this section, results are summarised first for responses measured using PsVNA against Omicron BA.1 and then against D614G. Further, data shown below for sero-response use the conventional definition for individual participant sero-response: nAb change on BD Day 29 from a Baseline (pre-dose 1 of primary series) level below the LLOQ to ≥4×LLOQ, or at least a 4 fold rise if the Baseline level was ≥LLOQ.

## Omicron BA.1

The GMR of Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age to Study P204 participants of the same age group for BD Day 29/Day 57 nAb levels against Omicron BA.1 was 12.085 (95% CI: 10.715, 13.631). The SRR difference was 14.2% (95% CI: 11.1%, 17.5%) (Efficacy Table 8). Thus, the superiority criterion for GMR (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;1.0) and the non-inferiority criterion for SRR difference (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt; 5%) were met.

Results of this analysis were consistent with the results of a prior interim analysis (05 Dec 2022 data cutoff) that demonstrated a GMR of 12.5 (95% CI: 11.0, 14.3) and an SRR difference of 13.6% (95% CI: 9.7%, 18.5%).

## D614G

The GMR of Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age to Study P204 participants of the same age group for BD Day 29/Day 57 nAb levels against D614G was 3.049 (95% CI: 2.725, 3.411). The SRR difference was 0.5% (95% CI: -0.7%, 1.6%) (Table 8). Thus, the non-inferiority criterion for GMR (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;0.667) and the non-inferiority criterion for SRR difference (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt; -10%) were met.

Results for sensitivity analyses for GMR and SRR, calculated by excluding participants who had SARSCoV-2 infection up to the analysis time point (BD Day 29 for Study P306 Part 2 and Day 57 for Study P204) in the PPIS-Neg group, revealed no meaningful differences compared to the primary analyses.

## Subgroup Analyses

## Primary Immunogenicity Analysis by Pre-BD SARS-CoV-2 Status

Vaccination induced an increase in nAbs, and almost all participants achieved sero-response regardless of SARS-CoV-2 status at Baseline (a majority of participants [65.7%] were pre-BD SARS-CoV-2 negative). As expected, pre-BD nAb levels were higher among participants with evidence of previous SARS-CoV-2 infection than among those who were negative for previous SARS-CoV-2 infection. Results showed that the mRNA-1273.214 BD induced a robust rise in BD Day 29 nAbs against both the Omicron BA.1 and D614G strains compared to the mRNA-1273 primary series, regardless of pre-BD SARS-CoV-2 status (Efficacy Table 9).

## Pseudovirus Neutralising Antibody Analyses by Other Subgroups

No meaningful differences were observed for nAb levels against both the Omicron BA.1 and D614G strains for the following examined subgroups: pre-BD as Baseline and by age subgroup, additional subgroups (sex [male, female], race (White, Black, others), ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Non-Hispanic or Latino or missing), race and ethnicity (White non-Hispanic or missing, Communities of Color), obesity, and obesity subgroup [non-obesity or missing]), and intake of pain or fever medications within 72 hours after dosing (after BD in Study P306 Part 2 and after Dose 2 in Study P204).

## Exploratory Endpoint - Long term Immunogenicity

The mRNA-1273.214 BD demonstrated persistent immunogenicity against both the Omicron BA.1 and D614G strains at 6 months (BD Day 181) after the BD. In the PPIS-Neg, nAb levels at BD Day 181 remained elevated compared to Baseline levels against both Omicron BA.1 (250.3 [95% CI: 217.4, 288.2]; n=227) and D614G (1338.4 [95% CI: 1185.1, 1511.5]; n=223). The GMFR was 6.6 (95% CI: 5.8, 7.4) and 3.6 (95% CI: 3.2, 4.0), respectively. The SRRs against Omicron BA.1 and D614G at BD Day 181 were 72.4% (95% CI: 66.1%, 78.2%; n=163) and 43.2% (95% CI: 36.6%, 50.0%; n=96), respectively.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

As a sensitivity analysis for the exploratory immunogenicity analysis, GMC was calculated by excluding participants who had SARS-CoV-2 infection up to the analysis timepoint (BD Day 181). Results indicate that nAb responses (GMC) against Omicron BA.1 and D614G through 6 months after the mRNA-1273.214 BD waned over time but remained above Baseline levels, regardless of intercurrent SARS-CoV-2 infection.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

The mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 µg) elicited a substantial increase in antibody levels against Omicron XBB.1.5 in previously vaccinated participants aged 6 months through 5 years. The analysis was based on the PPIS. Sero-response was observed in a large proportion of participants.

## Secondary Immunogenicity Results

## Geometric Mean Value

Overall, a substantial rise in nAb titres was observed following a single 25 µg BD. The nAb GM value was 2827.4 (95% CI: 2389.9, 3345.1) on BD-Day 29, corresponding to a 13.5-fold rise (95% CI: 11.4, 15.9) from pre-BD values (210.2 [95% CI: 176.6, 250.1]) (Efficacy Table 10).

## Sero-response Rate

Sero-response at BD-Day 29 was achieved by 79.0% (95% CI: 73.4%, 83.8%) of participants, with no meaningful differences observed between age subgroups (≥6 months to &lt;2 years and ≥2 to &lt;6 years) (Efficacy Table 10).

## Subgroup Analyses

No meaningful differences were observed between age subgroups (≥6 months to &lt;2 years and ≥2 to &lt;6 years). Substantial increases in nAbs relative to Baseline were observed regardless of pre-booster SARSCoV-2 antibody status. No meaningful differences were observed in serum Ab GM value and SRR against SARS-CoV-2 when assessed by gender, race, ethnicity, race/ethnicity, and obesity.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 years) and 2 dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Primary Endpoint: Immunogenicity Analyses to Infer Vaccine Effectiveness

The primary immunogenicity endpoint to infer effectiveness of single 25 µg dose of mRNA-1273.815 was based on the GMR.

In this section, the results are summarised for responses measured by PsVNA against Omicron XBB.1.5.

The primary immunogenicity analysis demonstrated that a single 25 µg dose of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort A PPIS-Pos elicited a non-inferior immune response against XBB.1.5 compared to a 25 µg 2-dose series of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort B PPIS-Neg. The GMR of nAb levels was 1.195 (95% CI: 0.800, 1.784), meeting the pre-specified non-inferiority criterion (lower bound of the 95% CI of the GMR &gt;0.667) (Efficacy Table 11).

2 negative through Day 57 (excluding those with evidence of intercurrent SARS-CoV-2 infection for Cohort B). The sensitivity analyses showed no meaningful differences compared to the primary analyses.

## Subgroup Analyses

No meaningful differences were observed in subgroup analyses by sex, race, ethnicity, race/ethnicity, and obesity status.

No meaningful differences were observed in nAb values against Omicron XBB.1.5 between age subgroups (6 months to &lt;12 months and 12 months to &lt;2 years) or Baseline antibody value subgroups (&lt;LLOQ and ≥LLOQ) in Cohort B.

## Secondary Immunogenicity Results

The secondary immunogenicity endpoints to assess the immunogenicity of a single 25 μg dose of mRNA1273.815 on Day 29 against Omicron XBB.1.5 in Cohort A PPIS-Pos to that on Day 57 in Cohort B PPISNeg after two 25 μg doses of mRNA-1273.815 were based on the SRR.

## Comparison of SRR Between Cohort A PPIS-Pos and Cohort B PPIS-Neg

Overall, the SRR against Omicron XBB.1.5 was achieved by 73.4% (95% CI: 65.2%, 80.5%) and 96.1% (95% CI: 88.9%, 99.2%) participants in Cohort A PPIS-Pos (Day 29) and Cohort B PPIS-Neg (Day 57), respectively. The SRR difference (Cohort A PPIS-Pos-Cohort B PPIS-Neg) was -22.7% (95% CI: -31.3%, -13.5%). The non-inferiority criterion for SRR difference (lower bound of the 95% CI &gt;-10%) was not met (Efficacy Table 12). Based on observations from prior clinical studies, it was expected that individuals who are seronegative at Baseline readily demonstrate a brisk sero-response (achieving a 4-fold rise) compared to individuals who are seropositive. Given this difference, it is reassuring that a majority of Cohort A participants achieved sero-response. Further, when using a 2-fold rise threshold (as opposed to a 4-fold rise) to define sero-response, 83.1% of Cohort A PPIS-Pos participants with high nAbs at Baseline (≥4 times LLOQ) demonstrated sero-response. These results indicate that a single dose of 25 µg can induce a robust immune response in most participants aged 2 years through 4 years.

Results for sensitivity analyses for SRR, calculated by comparing the sero-response in Cohort A PPIS-Pos participants with Cohort B PPIS-Neg participants who remained SARS-CoV-2 negative through Day 57 (excluding those with evidence of intercurrent SARS CoV-2 infection for Cohort B), showed no meaningful differences compared to the primary analyses.

No meaningful differences in SRRs against Omicron XBB.1.5 were observed in subgroup analyses by Cohort B age subgroup (6 months to &lt;12 months and 12 months to &lt;2 years) and Baseline antibody values (&lt;LLOQ and ≥LLOQ) from the overall analyses (Table 12).

## Comparison of GM Values and SRR Between Cohort A PPIS and Cohort B PPIS

In the PPIS, at Baseline, the nAb GM values were 151.4 (95% CI: 116.3, 197.0) and 90.7 (95% CI: 74.6, 110.4) in Cohort A and Cohort B, respectively. The model-based nAb GM values were 1975.5 (95% CI: 1593.2, 2449.6) and 3768.9 (95% CI: 3278.2, 4333.0) in Cohort A on Day 29 and Cohort B on Day 57, respectively. Since this analysis included participants who were Baseline SARS-CoV-2 positive in Cohort B (while the primary endpoint analysis included only participants who were Baseline negative), the titres after receipt of 2 doses were predictably higher than Cohort A. The GMR (Day 29 Cohort A/Day 57 Cohort B) was 0.524 (95% CI: 0.406, 0.677).

Among all participants regardless of Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status (i.e., PPIS), sero-response was achieved by 73.6% (95% CI: 65.6%, 80.6%) and 89.0% (95% CI: 85.3%, 92.1%) participants in Cohort A and Cohort B, respectively. The SRR difference (Cohort A-Cohort B) was -15.4% (95% CI: -23.8%, -8.0%).

## Exploratory Immunogenicity Results

The exploratory immunogenicity endpoints assessed immune responses against Omicron XBB.1.5 on Day 29 in Cohort A, measured by SRR using definition 'b' that was similar to definition 'a' except for participants with Baseline nAb levels ≥LLOQ (at least 2-fold rise versus at least 4-fold rise).

In Cohort A, using definition 'b', 84.2% (95% CI: 77.0%, 89.8%) and 84.0% (95% CI: 77.0%, 89.6%) of participants achieved sero-response in the PPIS-Pos (Table 12) and PPIS, respectively.

## 6.3. Discussion

With this submission the MAH is providing the final CSRs from study P306 parts 1 - 4. Study P306 was an open-label, Phase 3 study to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of Spikevax variant-containing formulations (mRNA-1273.214, encoding ancestral Wuhan-Hu-1 and omicron B.1.1.529 [BA.1], and mRNA-1273.815, encoding Omicron XBB.1.5) in individuals 6 months through 5 years of age. The study was initiated in June 2022, initially consisting of only Part 1 and Part 2 that were conducted in the U.S and completed in June 2024. Part 3 and Part 4 were added in November 2023 in response to evolving SARS-CoV-2 epidemiology impacting paediatric dosing regimen and were completed in October 2025. Parts 3 and 4 were conducted in the U.S, Argentina, Colombia and Panama and in the U.S, Dominican Republic and Panama, respectively.

Initially the purpose of study P306 was to evaluate the immune response against SARS-CoV-2 variants of concern (VOC) after primary-series vaccination or booster dose of variant adapted vaccines in children 6

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

months through 5 years of age. Since a substantial number, and later on, the majority of participants was already SARS-CoV-2 positive prior to study vaccination the purpose of this submission was changed to update sections 4.2, 4.8 and 5.1 of the SmPC with a change to the posology for the 2 years through 4 years age group, based on results from the final CSR for mRNA-1273-P306 for Spikevax, while fulfilling Article 46 of the Paediatric Regulation. The purpose of the change of the posology for the 2 years through 4 years age group was no longer pursued after the first round of question and will remain unchanged in the SmPC. Safety and immunogenicity results from study P306 Part 1 - 4 are provided. Assays used in the respective parts were validated which is acknowledged. Demographics are acceptable for the submission purpose. Analyses were performed based on participants in the P306 PPIS. Immunogenicity analysis for part 2 is based on PPIS-neg results. Given the increase in seroprevalence in the general population at the time of study P306 conduct, the P306 PPIS included all participants regardless of whether they were baseline SARS-CoV-2 negative or positive. Accordingly, the PPIS was defined in the same manner for the historical comparator Study P204 for parts 1, 3 and 4. However, with varying numbers of SARS-CoV-2 positive participants in P306 vs P204 there might be a bias in the comparison of nAb titres that needs to be noted. Definitions of non-inferiority and superiority were the same as have been used earlier to infer effectiveness, the same as for the definition of the seroresponse. In part 1 the mRNA-1273.214 primary series induced an increase in nAbs regardless of SARS-CoV-2 infection status at Baseline. The pre-specified success criteria for the primary immunogenicity objective were met, thus establishing immunobridging to Study P204 and thereby inferring vaccine effectiveness. In part 2 the primary immunogenicity analyses demonstrated that the immune response against Omicron BA.1 elicited by the mRNA-1273.214 BD (10µg) in Study P306 Part 2 participants 6 months through 5 years of age was superior, and the immune response against D614G was non-inferior, compared to the immune response elicited by the mRNA-1273 2 dose primary series in Study P204 participants of the same age range. The primary immunogenicity analysis was based on the PPIS-Neg. In part 3 the mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 µg) elicited a substantial increase in antibody levels against Omicron XBB.1.5 in previously vaccinated participants aged 6 months through 5 years. The analysis was based on the PPIS. In these analyses not all pre-defined success criteria were met for SRR difference. This is impacted by the analysis group analysed (prior SARS-CoV-2 infection vs. SARS-CoV-2 negative), however, this finding is due to varying baseline titres and the ability to further elicit nAb titres. In the context of this submission this finding is not of relevance. While immunogenicity analyses from parts 1 through 3 are reassuring the use of variant-adapted mRNA1273 vaccines as a 2-dose primary series regimen in SARS-CoV-2 naïve subjects and a single booster dose after SARS-CoV-2 primary-series vaccination, results from part 4 demonstrated that a single 25 µg dose of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort A PPIS-Pos elicited a non-inferior immune response against XBB.1.5 compared to a 25 µg 2-dose primary series of mRNA-1273.815 in Cohort B PPIS-Neg. What has not been demonstrated with the submitted results is the comparison of a single booster dose in subjects 2 - 4 years of age that have not been vaccinated previously and no history of SARS-CoV-2 infection versus 2age group can be considered seropositive for SARS-CoV-2. This was requested to be further

dose primary series vaccination. The MAH argues that sero-epidemiology has changed and subjects in this substantiated, and/or non-inferiority of a single booster dose in 2 - 4 year old SARS-CoV-2 naïve children

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

without prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination versus a 2-dose primary series should be demonstrated. The MAH agreed in its response that the submitted data do not demonstrate non-inferiority of a single dose compared with a 2 dose primary series in SARS-CoV-2-naïve children aged 2 to &lt;5 years. The MAH further responded that it will no longer pursue the proposed single-dose posology for children aged 2 to &lt;5 years and proposes to maintain the currently approved 2-dose primary series for all children in this age group, which is accepted.

## 7. Clinical Safety aspects

The MAH has developed mRNA-1273 (SPIKEVAX) and its variant containing formulations as vaccines to prevent COVID-19, the disease caused by SARS-CoV-2. On 18 Dec 2020, mRNA-1273 100 µg 2 dose primary series received an EUA in the US for active immunisation to prevent COVID-19 in individuals ≥18 years of age and subsequently received a biologics licensing application approval on 31 Jan 2022. Subsequent to the initial EUA in December 2020, mRNA-1273 was authorized in many countries globally for use in adolescents (12 years through 17 years of age) and children (6 months through 11 years of age). Additionally, approval/authorisation in ages 6 months and older for the variant containing formulations of mRNA-1273 (SPIKEVAX Bivalent Original and Omicron BA.1 subvariant [mRNA-1273.214; authorisation outside the US], SPIKEVAX Bivalent Original and Omicron BA.4/BA.5 subvariant [mRNA1273.222], SPIKEVAX XBB.1.5 [mRNA-1273.815]), SPIKEVAX JN.1 [mRNA-1273.167; authorisation outside of the US and Canada], SPIKEVAX KP.2 [mRNA-1273.712]), and SPIKEVAX LP.8.1 (mRNA1273.251) have been granted.

The current approval/authorisation in European Union/United Kingdom is as per the dosing posology presented below:

## Study mRNA-1273-P306

Study P306 was an open-label, Phase 3 study to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of mRNA-1273 variant-containing formulations (mRNA-1273.214 and mRNA-1273.815) for SARS-CoV-2 in individuals 6 months through 5 years of age. The study was initiated in Jun 2022, initially consisting of only Part 1 and Part 2 and was completed in Jun 2024. Part 3 and Part 4 were added in Nov 2023 in response to evolving SARS-CoV-2 epidemiology impacting pediatric dosing regimen and were completed in Aug 2025 and Jan 2026, respectively. Each part evaluated the epidemiologically relevant vaccine formulation at that time, as below:

- Part 1 evaluated mRNA-1273.214 administered as a 2-dose primary series (25 µg/dose; 2 doses, 28 days apart) in participants 6 months through 5 years of age not previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2.
- Part 2 evaluated mRNA-1273.214 administered as a single 10 µg BD in participants 6 months through 5 years of age who previously received mRNA-1273 25 µg as a 2 dose primary series.
- Part 3 evaluated mRNA-1273.815 administered as a single 25 µg BD in participants 6 months through 5 years of age previously vaccinated with an authorized/approved COVID-19 vaccine primary series regimen.
- Part 4 included 2 cohorts.
- Cohort A (Part 4A) evaluated mRNA-1273.815 administered as a single 25 µg dose in participants 2 years through 4 years of age not previously vaccinated against SARS CoV2.
- Cohort B (Part 4B) evaluated mRNA-1273.815 administered as two 25 µg doses 28 days apart in participants 6 months through 23 months of age not previously vaccinated against SARS-CoV-2. The results from Cohort B were used as an active control group to compare those results obtained for Cohort A (Part 4A; treatment group).

This submission provides safety data for mRNA-1273 formulations from Study P306.

Additional details on study design, vaccines used in the study, vaccine exposure, database lock dates, and duration of follow up from receipt of any dose are presented below.

## 7.1. Methods - analysis of data submitted

## Analysis Populations

Analysis populations for Study P306 are defined below.

In addition, for Part 1 and Part 4B, this set was defined for each dose separately - First Study Vaccination Solicited Safety Set (all participants in the Solicited Safety Set who received the first dose) and Second Study Vaccination Solicited Safety Set (all participants in the Solicited Safety Set who received the second dose). ## Solicited Adverse Reactions

Solicited ARs were assessed using a pre-specified list of solicited local and systemic AR terms that were reported by participants daily via eDiary during the 7 days following each dose (i.e., the day of dosing [primary series or BD] and 6 subsequent days). Solicited ARs that persisted beyond Day 7 were recorded until resolution or until the next dose, if applicable.

Severity grading (Grade 0 through Grade 4) of solicited local and systemic ARs occurred automatically based on participant entry into the eDiary according to the grading scales presented in Table 16, modified from the Toxicity Grading Scale for Healthy Adult and Adolescent Volunteers Enrolled in Preventative Vaccine Clinical Trials (DHHS 2007).

Two versions of this scale were used in Study P306 Parts 1, 2, and 3, one that applied to participants 37 months of age and older, and one that applied to participants 6 through 36 months of age.

In Study P306 Part 4, all participants, regardless of age, were graded for solicited ARs using the same toxicity scale (6 months through 36 months).

The assessments for solicited systemic ARs differed based on age: fever, irritability/crying, sleepiness, and loss of appetite were assessed in participants 6 months through 36 months of age, and fever, headache, fatigue, myalgia, arthralgia, nausea/vomiting, and chills were assessed in participants 37 months of age and older. For the assessment of fever, the temperature ranges used for toxicity grading also differed depending on age. The same assessments were used for solicited local ARs; however, grading parameters differed for injection site swelling and injection site erythema based on age.
