## Unsolicited Adverse Events

An unsolicited AE was any AE reported by the participant that was not specified as a solicited AR in the study protocol or was specified as a solicited AR but started outside the protocol defined period for reporting solicited ARs (i.e., with onset after Day 7 of dosing). Initially, any solicited ARs with onset up to Day 7 and meeting the following criteria were also reported as unsolicited AEs if they: lasted beyond Day 7 after dosing; resulted in a visit to an HCP (i.e., met the definition of an MAAE); were serious (i.e., met the definition of an SAE); and/or led to discontinuation of study vaccine or study participation. Additionally, per study protocol, confirmed cases of COVID-19, based on COVID-19 event eligible symptoms and confirmed by RT-PCR laboratory test results or based on home testing kits when these became available, were also reported as unsolicited AEs.

Following analyses performed in accordance with FDA requests, data presented in this submission reflect the following changes in Study P306:

- Solicited ARs starting within 7 days after dosing (i.e., the day of dosing and 6 subsequent days) that persisted beyond Day 7 were no longer classified as unsolicited AEs, unless the solicited AR was serious (meeting the definition of an SAE).
- Solicited ARs resulting in a visit to an HCP (meeting the definition of MAAEs) were no longer classified as unsolicited AEs, unless the solicited AR was serious.
- Solicited ARs leading to study vaccine discontinuation and/or study participation discontinuation were no longer classified as unsolicited AEs, unless the solicited AR was serious.
- AE terms relating to COVID-19 or SARS-CoV-2 were considered clinical endpoints and were no longer considered safety endpoints for Part 1 and Part 2; thus, events of COVID-19 or SARS-CoV2 were not summarised as AEs in Part 1 and Part 2. Active surveillance for COVID-19 was not included in Part 3 and Part 4 of the study; therefore, COVID-19 and related events were captured as AEs in Part 3 and Part 4.

Severity (mild, moderate, or severe) and causality (related or not related) of unsolicited AEs were summarised per the Investigator's assessment according to the guidelines for these assessments prespecified in the study protocols.

Unsolicited AEs were collected up to 28 days after each dose (i.e., the day of dosing and 27 subsequent days). SAEs (including deaths), MAAEs, AESIs, unsolicited AEs leading to discontinuation of study vaccine (as applicable), and/or study participation were collected throughout the study period in all parts of the study.

## Serious Adverse Events

An AE (including an AR) was considered an SAE if, in the view of either the Investigator or Sponsor, it led to any of the following outcomes: resulted in death, was life threatening, required inpatient hospitalisation or prolongation of existing hospitalisation, resulted in persistent or significant disability/incapacity or substantial disruption of the ability to conduct normal life functions, or was a congenital anomaly/birth defect or any medically important event. SAEs (including deaths) were collected from Day 1 and through the entire study period.

## Medically Attended Adverse Events

An MAAE was defined as any unsolicited AE that led to an unscheduled visit to an HCP. This included visits to a study site for unscheduled assessments (e.g., abnormal laboratory test result, evaluation of an

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

illness) and visits to HCPs external to the study site (e.g., urgent care, primary care physician). Investigators reviewed unsolicited AEs for the occurrence of any MAAE.

Participants with suspected COVID-19 were required per protocol to visit the study site for evaluation. For Part 1 and Part 2 of Study P306 (per protocol), all confirmed COVID-19 cases (as well as any other AEs associated with an illness visit) after the administration of study vaccine were recorded as MAAEs. Such MAAEs - along with relevant concomitant medications and details about severity, seriousness, and outcome - were to be reported immediately to the Sponsor.

However, for Part 1 and Part 2, the data presented in this submission represent results from an analysis conducted per FDA request, where all COVID-19 related events were to be treated as clinical endpoints and not reported as unsolicited AEs. Therefore, remapping was performed, treating any AE terms containing \"COVID\" or \"SARS-CoV-2\" as clinical events in datasets and in analysis instead of AEs.

## Adverse Events of Special Interest

An AESI was an unsolicited AE (serious or nonserious) of scientific and medical concern specific to the Sponsor's product or program for which ongoing monitoring, immediate notification by the Investigator to the Sponsor, and documentation in the form of a case narrative were required. Such events may have required further investigation to characterize and understand them.

## Multisystem Inflammatory Syndrome in Children

Investigators were instructed to report clinical signs/symptoms consistent with the CDC case definition of MIS-C as an AESI. MIS-C was also to be considered in any pediatric death with evidence of SARS-CoV-2 infection, as well as in participants fulfilling full or partial criteria for Kawasaki's disease if meeting the case definition for MIS-C.

## Protocol-defined Adverse Events of Special Interest

In addition to designation of MIS-C as an AESI, other AESIs selected for Study P306 were medical concepts that may be related to COVID-19 or were of interest in COVID-19 vaccine safety surveillance. A full list of the AESIs assessed for the study is provided in Study P306 Protocol Table 12.

## Programmed Standardized MedDRA Queries

All reported unsolicited AEs (regardless of Investigator's assessment as AESI) were queried using MedDRA SMQs (narrow and broad scope) to identify AESIs. The SMQs selected represent medical events that were assessed by global experts and regulatory agencies as hypothetically relevant to the COVID-19 vaccines. The SMQs for Study P306 included Anaphylactic reaction, Angioedema, Arthritis, Convulsions, Demyelination, Hypersensitivity, Peripheral neuropathy, Vasculitis, Central nervous system vascular disorders, Embolic and thrombotic events, Hearing and vestibular disorders, Cardiomyopathy, Hematopoietic cytopenia, Ischemic heart disease, Cardiac failure, and Cardiac arrhythmias. The SMQs of Cardiomyopathy and Hypersensitivity were critically reviewed for potential myocarditis and/or pericarditis and anaphylaxis events, respectively.

## Myocarditis and Pericarditis

Investigators were instructed to report any cases of suspected, probable, or confirmed myocarditis, pericarditis, or myopericarditis as AESIs due to the previously identified safety signal of an association between myocarditis and/or pericarditis and COVID-19 mRNA vaccines, particularly in adolescents and young adults (real world use data). In order to enhance sensitivity for identifying cases of myocarditis and/or pericarditis after the safety signal emerged, the safety call script in each study was revised to specifically solicit symptoms suggestive of myocarditis and/or pericarditis at pre-specified timepoints. Any

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

cases of myocarditis, pericarditis, or myopericarditis reported were to be adjudicated by a CEAC, made up of pediatric and adult cardiologists, to determine if the event met CDC case definition of probable or confirmed myocarditis or pericarditis events, or as \"not a charter defined case\".

Additionally, supplemental measures to search for unrecognized myocarditis and/or pericarditis cases were employed. The safety dataset was interrogated for unsolicited AEs compatible with signs, symptoms, laboratory investigations, or procedural findings that might indicate potential cases. Two overlapping approaches were used to interrogate all events using: (1) the narrow and broad Cardiomyopathy SMQ, and (2) analysis of PTs reported and potentially indicative of myocarditis and pericarditis using a customized MedDRA query (based on the CDC working case definition).

Any potential myocarditis and/or pericarditis cases identified by these reviews would also be adjudicated by the CEAC. Reports of chest pain and other nonspecific symptoms were not sent for adjudication if there was sufficient supporting information, e.g., electrocardiogram/troponin or other test results not indicative of myocarditis and/or pericarditis.

Note: since specific symptoms for myocarditis and pericarditis were solicited as part of safety calls and follow up, events such as chest pain and dyspnea were sometimes reported as AESIs even though not meeting the protocol defined AESI criteria. This enhanced collection may also have introduced some bias in reporting practices around these events.

## Anaphylaxis

Investigators were instructed to report events of anaphylaxis as an AESI. The narrow and broad SMQ for Hypersensitivity was used to identify events of hypersensitivity, including anaphylaxis, which were subsequently reviewed.

## 7.2. Results

## Overall Extent of Exposure and Duration of Follow-up

Inclusion/exclusion criteria were designed to largely mimic the population expected to receive vaccine in the real world, i.e., healthy children as well as children with medically stable underlying diseases were eligible to participate.

Brief summaries of exposure information and follow-up duration for each part of Study P306 (Parts 1 through 4) are provided in the following, and in Table 17and Table 18, respectively.

- In Part 1, mRNA-1273.214 25 µg was administered as a 2 dose primary series in participants 6 months through 5 years of age. A total of 391 participants received Dose 1 and 378 participants received both doses. Throughout Part 1, the median duration of follow-up was 390.0 days from Dose 1 and 358.0 days from Dose 2. A total of 360 participants (92.1%) and 327 participants (83.6%) had ≥6 months (i.e., ≥168 days) and ≥12 months (i.e., ≥336 days) of safety follow-up after Dose 2 of mRNA-1273.214, respectively.
- In Part 2, mRNA-1273.214 10 µg was administered as a BD to 532 participants 6 months through 5 years of age who were previously enrolled in Study P204 and had received mRNA-1273 25 µg 2 dose primary series. Six participants received a 25 µg BD of mRNA-1273.214 instead of the 10 µg dose in error and 1 participant received a mRNA-1273.214 dose of &lt;10 µg as a few drops of the vaccine could not be injected due to the participant movement. Together, these 539 participants were assessed as part of the mRNA-1273.214 10 µg BD analysis in Study P306 Part 2. The median duration of follow-up after the mRNA-1273.214 BD was 184.0 days. A total of 524 (97.2%) participants had ≥6 months (i.e., ≥168 days) of safety follow-up after the BD.

[Note: \"Study P204\" refers to Study mRNA-1273 P204, a Phase 2/3, dose escalation, age deescalation (Part 1, open label) and randomized, observer blind, placebo controlled expansion study (Part 2) to evaluate the safety, reactogenicity, and effectiveness of mRNA-1273 (primary series and BD) and safety of mRNA-1273.214 (BD) in children 6 months through 11 years of age, assessing up to 3 dose levels (25 μg, 50 μg, and 100 μg) of mRNA-1273 in the primary series.]

- In Part 3, mRNA-1273.815 25 µg was administered as a BD to 274 participants 6 months through 5 years of age. The median study duration after the BD was 181.0 days (range: 11 to 209 days). A total of 266 participants (97.1%) were followed for ≥168 days after BD.
- In Part 4A (Part 4 Cohort A), mRNA-1273.815 25 μg was administered as a single dose to a total of 199 participants 2 to &lt;5 years of age. The median study duration after study vaccination was 92.0 days (range: 64 to 114 days). Participants were followed for approximately 3 months after dosing, with the majority (198/199 participants [99.5%]) followed for ≥84 days from dosing.
- In Part 4B (Part 4 Cohort B), mRNA-1273.815 25 µg was administered as a 2 dose primary series to participants 6 months to &lt;2 years of age. All 399 participants received the first study vaccination, while 386 participants received both doses. The median study duration was 118 days (range: 8 to 191 days) from the first study vaccination and 86 days (range: 0 to 104 days) from the second study vaccination. Most participants (238/399 [59.6%]) were followed for ≥84 days after the second study vaccination.

Sources: Study P306 Final CSR Part 1 and Part 2, Table 14.1.5.1 and Table 14.1.5.2; Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Table 14.1.5.3; Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A, Table 14.1.5.3.4.

## Demographics and Other Characteristics

## Baseline Demographics and Characteristics

The table below presents a tabular summary of demographics and baseline characteristics for all 4 Parts of Study P306.

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Overall, there was a similar distribution of male (202/391 [51.7%]) and female (189/391 participants [48.3%]), 175/391 (44.8%) were White/non Hispanic, and 216/391 (55.2%) were of race and ethnicity other than White and non Hispanic. The median age was 2.00 years, 78/391 participants (19.9%) were obese, and 295/391 participants (75.4%) had a positive SARS-CoV-2 status at Baseline. Of note, participant enrollment for Study P306 Part 1 started on 21 Jun 2022 after the Omicron BA.1 wave in the US, and before the population age group (6 months through 5 years) in the study received access to COVID-19 vaccines, which likely contributed to the high baseline positivity.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Demographic and baseline characteristics in the 2 age subgroups (6 months through 23 months and 2 years through 5 years) were generally comparable to the overall population. No clinically relevant differences were observed between the age subgroups.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Of the 539 participants who received the BD, 276 (51.2%) were male, 395 (73.3%) were White/ nonHispanic, and 143/539 participants (26.5%) were of race and ethnicity other than White and nonHispanic. The median age was 3.00 years, 170/539 participants (31.5%) had a positive pre-BD SARSCoV-2 status, and 70/539 participants (13.0%) were obese.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Overall, the median age of participants was 3.00 years (range: 0.7 to 5.0 years) with a balanced proportion of male (136/274 [49.6%]) and female (138/274 [50.4%]) participants. Age distribution of enrolled participants was balanced across the eligible age ranges, and race and ethnicity were reflective of the geographic regions that enrolled in the study. The pre-BD SARS-CoV-2 status was positive in 230/274 participants (83.9%), which was expected given the endemicity of SARS-CoV-2 and known rise in seropositivity in the general population, including children.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years To &lt;5 Years):

Overall, the proportion of male (100/199 [50.3%]) and female (99/199 [49.7%]) participants was balanced. Most participants were Hispanic or Latino in ethnicity (138/199 [69.3%]), while 73/199 participants (36.7%) were identified as being a race other than White, Black, Asian, Native American, or Pacific Islander. The median age of the participants was 3.00 years. Overall, 192/199 participants (96.5%) had a positive SARS-CoV-2 status at Baseline.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months To &lt;2 Years):

Overall, the proportion of male participants (208/399 [52.1%]) and female participants (191/399 [47.9%]) was balanced. Most participants were Hispanic or Latino in ethnicity (338/399 [84.7%]), while 225/399 participants (56.4%) were identified as being a race other than White, Black, Asian, Native American, or Pacific Islander. The median age of the participants was 1.00 years. Overall, the Baseline SARS-CoV-2 status was negative in 93/399 participants (23.3%), positive in 305/399 participants (76.4%), and missing in 1 participant.

f For Study P306 Part 2 and Part 3, pre-BD SARS-CoV-2 status was positive if there was immunologic or virologic evidence of prior COVID-19, defined as positive RT-PCR test or positive Elecsys result on or before BD-Day 1, and negative defined as a negative RT-PCR test and negative Elecsys result on or before BD-Day 1.

## Medical History

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

A total of 181/391 participants (46.3%) reported at least 1 medical history condition. The most frequently reported (&gt;5.0% of participants) medical history conditions were seasonal allergy (47/391 [12.0%]) and eczema (24/391 [6.1%]).

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

A total of 248/539 participants (46.0%) reported at least 1 medical history condition. The most frequently reported (&gt;5.0% of participants) medical history conditions were seasonal allergy (81/539 [15.0%]) and eczema (29/539 [5.4%]).

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

A total of 101/274 participants (36.9%) had at least 1 medical history condition. The most frequently reported (≥2% overall) medical history conditions were seasonal allergy (21/274 participants [7.7%]), eczema (12/274 participants [4.4%]), asthma (7/274 participants [2.6%]), and bronchiolitis, otitis media, and autism spectrum disorder (6/274 participants each [2.2%]).

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years to &lt;5 Years):

A total of 51/199 participants (25.6%) had at least 1 medical history condition. The most frequently reported medical history conditions in Cohort A were asthma (8/199 [4.0%]), nasopharyngitis and seasonal allergy (6/199 participants [3.0%] each), COVID-19 (4/199 [2.0%]), and sepsis neonatal (3/199 [1.5%]).

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months to &lt;2 Years):

A total of 115/399 participants (28.8%) had at least 1 medical history condition. The most frequently reported medical history conditions in Cohort B were nasopharyngitis (23/399 [5.8%]), asymptomatic COVID-19 (11/399 [2.8%]), umbilical hernia (9/399 [2.3%]), and bronchiolitis (8/399 [2.0%]).

## Adverse Events

## Solicited Local and Systemic Adverse Reactions

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Solicited local ARs were reported for 137/391 participants (35.0%) and 133/377 participants (35.3%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2 of mRNA-1273.214, respectively. The most frequently reported solicited local AR was pain after Dose 1 and Dose 2 (125/391 [32.0%] and 126/377 participants [33.4%], respectively). Solicited local ARs were mainly Grade 1 or 2 in severity. Grade 3 solicited local ARs were reported for 3/391 (0.8%) and 2/377 participants (0.5%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2, respectively. Erythema (2/391 participants [0.5%]) was the most frequently reported Grade 3 solicited local AR after Dose 1 and pain (2/377 participants [0.5%]) was the only Grade 3 solicited local AR reported after Dose 2. No Grade 4 solicited local ARs were reported.

Solicited systemic ARs were reported for 174/391 (44.5%) and 163/377 participants (43.2%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2 of mRNA-1273.214, respectively. The most frequently reported solicited systemic ARs after Dose 1 and Dose 2 were irritability/crying (82/180 [45.6%] and 76/188 participants [40.4%], respectively), sleepiness (57/180 [31.7%] and 45/188 participants [23.9%], respectively), and fatigue (51/176 [29.0%] and 46/174 participants [26.4%], respectively). Grade 3 solicited systemic ARs were reported for 4/391 participants (1.0%) and 10/377 participants (2.7%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2, respectively. Fever (2/391 and 2/377 participants [0.5%] each) after Dose 1 and Dose 2 and loss of appetite and irritability/crying (4/188 [2.1%] and 3/188 participants [1.6%], respectively) after Dose 2 were the most frequently reported Grade 3 solicited systemic ARs. One solicited systemic AR of Grade 4 (fever) was reported for 1/377 participants (0.3%) after Dose 2.

Between age subgroup comparisons showed incidences of solicited ARs after any dose to be lower in the 2 years through 5 years age subgroup than those in the 6 months through 23 months age subgroup. The most pronounced differences were noted in solicited systemic ARs, which were reported in 81/130 (62.3%) and 67/125 participants (53.6%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2, respectively, in the 6 months through 23 months age subgroup compared with 93/261 (35.6%) and 96/252 participants (38.1%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2, respectively, in the 2 years through 5 years age subgroup. No Grade 4 solicited ARs were reported in the 6 months through 23 months age subgroup, while in the 2 years through 5 years age subgroup, 1 participant experienced Grade 4 fever after Dose 2.

Source: Study P306 Final CSR Part 1 and Part 2, Table 14.3.1.1.1.2.1.1.

## Onset and Duration of Solicited Adverse Reactions

The onset of most solicited local and systemic ARs was within the first 1 to 2 days after both the doses. Solicited ARs within 30 minutes after Dose 1 and Dose 2 were reported in a total of 81/391 (20.7%) and 69/377 participants (18.3%), respectively. Within 30 minutes after both doses, the most frequently reported solicited local AR was injection site pain (60/391 participants [15.3%] after Dose 1 and 60/377 participants [15.9%] after Dose 2), and the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR was irritability/crying (27/180 participants [15.0%] after Dose 1 and 14/188 participants [7.4%] after Dose 2). Solicited local ARs had a median onset of 1.0 day (range: 1 to 6 days) after Dose 1 and 1.0 days (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 2. Solicited systemic ARs had a median onset of 1.0 days (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 1 and 2.0 days (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 2. Pain, the most frequently reported solicited local AR after both doses, had a median onset of 1.0 days (range: 1 to 4 days) after Dose 1 and 1.0 days (range: 1 to 6 days) after Dose 2. Irritability/crying, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR, had a median onset of 1.0 day (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 1 and 2.0 days (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 2.

Solicited local ARs had a median duration of 1.0 days (range: 1 to 7 days) each after Dose 1 and Dose 2. Solicited systemic ARs had a median duration of 1.0 days (range: 1 to 23 days) after Dose 1 and 1.0 days (range: 1 to 14 days) after Dose 2.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

No solicited local ARs persisting beyond 7 days after either dose were reported. Solicited systemic ARs persisting beyond 7 days after Dose 1 and Dose 2 were reported for 3/391 participants (0.8%) after Dose 1 and 3/377 participants (0.8%) after Dose 2. All solicited systemic ARs persisting beyond 7 days after Dose 1 or Dose 2 were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, except for 1 Grade 4 fever in a 5 year old participant after Dose 2.

## Medication Use for Solicited Adverse Reactions of Pain or Fever

Medications taken to treat or prevent pain or fever after Dose 1 and Dose 2 were used by 74/391 participants (18.9%) and 94/377 participants (24.9%), respectively. Overall, after each dose, the use of medications to treat pain or fever was more common than their use for preventing pain or fever.

## Fevers Reported During the 7 Days After Any Dose

Fevers within 7 days of dosing were reported for 26/391 participants (6.6%) after Dose 1 and for 40/377 participants (10.6%) after Dose 2. Most fevers (5.4% of participants after Dose 1 and 9.5% of participants after Dose 2) were in the range of ≥38°C to &lt;39°C. Of note, 1 Grade 2 fever was recorded by the Investigator after Dose 1, but the temperature was not recorded in the eDiary. Higher fevers in the range of ≥39°C to ≤40°C were reported in 5/391 participants (1.3%) after Dose 1 and in 4/377 participants (1.1%) after Dose 2.

No fevers &gt;40°C were reported in the eDiary after any dose. However, 1 Grade 4 fever was reported by the Investigator (not in the eDiary) as a solicited AR after Dose 2. The Grade 4 fever (104.1°F/40.1°C) occurred 5 days after Dose 2 in a 5 year old participant. The fever was associated with exposure to a sick family member and with concurrent symptoms of nasal congestion, runny nose, cough, and myalgia (reported as an unsolicited AE of mild viral upper respiratory tract infection). The fever duration was 4 days and viral upper respiratory tract infection duration was 7 days.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

## Solicited Local and Systemic Adverse Reactions

A total of 371/539 participants (68.8%) reported at least 1 solicited AR during the 7 days after the BD, with solicited local and systemic ARs reported for 269/539 participants (49.9%) and 265/539 participants (49.2%), respectively. The most frequently reported solicited ARs were irritability/crying (121/228 participants [53.1%]), pain (243/539 participants [45.1%]), and fatigue (88/274 participants [32.1%]). Most of the solicited ARs were Grade 1 or 2 in severity. Few Grade 3 solicited ARs were reported (19/539 participants [3.5%]). No Grade 4 solicited ARs were reported.

Fever (any grade) during the 7 days after the BD was reported for 39/539 participants (7.2%). Most of the fever events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity. Grade 3 fever was reported in 3/539 participants (0.6%). No Grade 4 (&gt;40.0°C) fevers were reported.

## Onset and Duration of Solicited Adverse Reactions

The onset of most solicited local and systemic ARs was within the first 2 days after the BD. The incidence within 30 minutes after the BD was higher for solicited local ARs than for solicited systemic ARs (111/539 participants [20.6%] versus 54/539 participants [10.0%], respectively). The most frequently reported solicited local AR within 30 minutes after the BD was pain (105/539 participants [19.5%]), and the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR was irritability/crying (37/228 participants [16.2%]).

The median duration of solicited ARs was 1.0 days after the BD for solicited local ARs (range: 1 to 14 days), and 1.0 days after the BD for solicited systemic ARs (range: 1 to 7 days). Solicited local ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the BD were reported in 2/539 participants (0.4%), including Grade 1 axillary (or groin) swelling or tenderness and Grade 2 swelling (hardness) in 1 participant each. No solicited systemic ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the BD were reported.

## Medication Use for Solicited Adverse Reactions of Pain or Fever

Medications taken after the BD to treat pain or fever were used by 90/539 participants (16.7%) and those taken to prevent pain or fever were used by 29/539 participants (5.4%).

## Fevers Reported During the 7 Days After BD

Fevers within 7 days of the BD were reported in 39/539 participants (7.2%). Most fevers (32/539 participants [5.9%]) were in the range of ≥38°C to &lt;39°C. Higher fevers in the range of ≥39°C to ≤40°C were reported in 7/539 participants (1.3%). No fevers &gt;40°C were reported in the eDiary.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

## Solicited Local and Systemic Adverse Reactions

A total of 146/271 participants (53.9%) reported at least 1 solicited AR during the 7 days after the BD. Most solicited ARs were Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity. No Grade 4 solicited ARs were reported.

Solicited local ARs were reported by 108/271 participants (39.9%), and of these, the most frequently reported were pain (98/271 participants [36.2%]) followed by axillary (or groin) swelling or tenderness

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

(20/271 participants [7.4%]). Erythema was reported in 7/271 participants (2.6%) and swelling was reported in 9/271 participants (3.3%). Grade 3 solicited local ARs occurred in 4/271 participants (1.5%).

Solicited systemic ARs were reported by 104/271 participants (38.4%). In the &gt;36 months to &lt;6 years age subgroup, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR was fatigue (41/151 participants [27.2%]), followed by irritability/crying (10/61 participants [16.4%]), headache (22/151 participants [14.6%]), myalgia (20/151 participants [13.2%]), and fever (31/237 participants [13.1%]) and loss of appetite (8/61 participants [13.1%]). In the ≥6 to ≤36 months age subgroup, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR was irritability/crying (19/95 participants [20.0%]) followed by loss of appetite (17/95 participants [17.9%]), sleepiness (13/95 participants [13.7%]), and fever (13/109 participants [11.9%]). Grade 3 solicited systemic ARs occurred in 14/271 participants (5.2%).

Overall, there were few Grade 3 fevers (6/271 participants [2.2%]), which occurred in the older age subgroup only. There were no Grade 4 fevers.

Pain for participant age 6 to ≤36 months/37 months to &lt;6 years is injection site pain or tenderness/injection site pain.

Refer to Table 4 for toxicity grades for solicited adverse reactions.

Source: Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Table 14.3.1.1.2.1.3

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Onset and Duration of Solicited Adverse Reactions

Onset of most solicited ARs was within the first 2 days of the BD. A total of 41/271 participants (15.1%) reported a solicited AR within 30 minutes of study vaccination (at study clinic), 49/271 (18.1%) on Day 1 after study vaccination (at home), and 37/271 (13.7%) on Day 2. From Days 3 to 7, ≤3% of participants reported onset of solicited ARs each day. The median duration of solicited ARs was 2 days (range: 1 to 10 days).

Solicited local ARs had a median onset on Day 1 (range: 1 to 4 days) and median duration of 1 day (range: 1 to 8 days). Pain, the most frequently reported solicited local AR, had a median onset on Day 1 (range: 1 to 3 days) and a median duration of 1 day (range: 1 to 7 days).

Solicited systemic ARs had a median onset on Day 2 (range: 1 to 7 days) and median duration 1 day (range: 1 to 10 days). Fatigue, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR, had a median onset on Day 2 (range: 1 to 7 days) and a median duration of 1 day (range: 1 to 7 days).

Solicited local ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the BD occurred in 2/271 participants (0.7%), both of whom were in the ≥2 to &lt;6 years age subgroup. These were events of Grade 1 swelling that lasted for 8 days, and Grade 2 erythema that lasted for 9 days.

Solicited systemic ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the BD occurred in 2/271 participants (0.7%). These were events of Grade 2 sleepiness in a participant in the ≥6 months to &lt;2 years age subgroup and Grade 1 loss of appetite in a participant in the ≥2 years to &lt;6 years age subgroup. Both events lasted for 10 days.

## Medication Use for Solicited Adverse Reactions of Pain and Fever

Overall, 71/271 participants (26.2%) were administered medication for pain or fever after BD, with 64/271 participants (23.6%) using medication to treat pain or fever and 15/271 participants (5.5%) using medication to prevent pain or fever.

## Fevers Reported During the 7 Days After Booster Dose

Within 7 days of receiving the BD, 36/271 participants (13.3%) experienced fever. Among them, 30/271 participants (11.1%) had fevers between 38°C and &lt;39°C, while 6/271 participants (2.2%) had fevers ranging from 39°C to ≤40°C (all in the ≥2 years and &lt;6 years age subgroup). No participants had temperatures &gt;40°C.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Solicited Local and Systemic Adverse Reactions

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

Solicited local ARs were reported for 59/199 participants (29.6%) within 7 days after the single dose. Pain (55/199 participants [27.6%]) was the most frequently reported solicited local AR.

The highest proportion of solicited local ARs were Grade 1, reported for 51/199 participants (25.6%). Grade 3 solicited local ARs were reported for 3/199 participants (1.5%), with axillary (or groin) swelling or tenderness (3/199 participants [1.5%]) being the most frequently reported Grade 3 solicited local AR.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Solicited systemic ARs were reported for 47/199 participants (23.6%) within 7 days after the single dose. Sleepiness (25/199 participants [12.6%]) and irritability/crying (24/199 participants [12.1%]) were the most frequently reported solicited systemic ARs.

The highest proportion of solicited systemic ARs were Grade 1, which was reported for 29/199 participants (14.6%). Grade 3 solicited systemic ARs were reported for 6/199 participants (3.0%), with 2/199 participants (1.0%) each reporting fever, irritability/crying, and sleepiness and 1/199 participants (0.5%) reporting loss of appetite.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months Through &lt;2 Years)

The incidence of solicited local ARs was similar for participants in Cohort B after Dose 1 (69/399 participants [17.3%]) and Dose 2 (55/386 participants [14.2%]). The most frequently reported solicited local AR was pain, occurring in 55/399 participants (13.8%) after Dose 1 and 43/386 participants (11.1%) after Dose 2.

The highest proportion of solicited local ARs were Grade 1, reported for 64/399 participants (16.0%) after Dose 1 and 44/386 participants (11.4%) after Dose 2. Grade 3 solicited local ARs were reported for 1/399 participants (0.3%) and 5/386 participants (1.3%) after Dose 1 and Dose 2, respectively. Erythema (1/399 participants [0.3%]) was the only Grade 3 solicited local AR reported after Dose 1, while pain and erythema (2/386 participants [0.5%] each) were the most frequently reported Grade 3 solicited local ARs after Dose 2.

The incidence of solicited systemic ARs was similar for participants in Cohort B after Dose 1 (128/399 participants [32.1%]) and Dose 2 (105/386 participants [27.2%]). The most frequently reported solicited systemic AR was irritability/crying, occurring in 69/399 participants (17.3%) after Dose 1 and 50/386 participants (13.0%) after Dose 2.

The highest proportion of solicited systemic ARs were Grade 1, reported for 86/399 participants (21.6%) after Dose 1 and 67/386 participants (17.4%) after Dose 2. Grade 3 solicited systemic ARs were reported for 11/399 participants (2.8%) after Dose 1 and 11/386 participants (2.8%) after Dose 2. Loss of appetite (6/399 participants [1.5%] after Dose 1 and 7/386 participants [1.8%] after Dose 2) was the most frequently reported Grade 3 solicited systemic AR.

## Onset and Duration of Solicited Adverse Reactions

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

The onset of most solicited local ARs was within the first 2 days after dosing, with a median onset at Day 1 (range: 1 to 4 days). Pain, the most frequently reported solicited local AR, had a median onset at Day 1 (range: 1 to 5 days). The median duration of solicited local ARs was 1.0 days (range: 1 to 9 days).

The onset of most solicited systemic ARs was within the first 2 days after dosing, with a median onset at Day 2 (range: 1 to 7 days). Sleepiness, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR, had a median onset at Day 1 (range: 1 to 6 days). The median duration of solicited systemic ARs was 1.0 days (range: 1 to 8 days).

Solicited ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the study vaccination occurred in 1/199 participants (0.5%) who experienced a solicited local AR of pain, and solicited systemic ARs of irritability/crying, sleepiness, and loss of appetite, all of which lasted up to Day 9 and were Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months Through &lt;2 Years)

The onset of most solicited local ARs was within the first 2 days after each dose, with a median onset at Day 1 (range: 1 to 7 days) after both doses. Pain, the most frequently reported solicited local AR, had a median onset at Day 1 (range: 1 to 6 days) after both doses. Solicited local ARs had a median duration of 1.0 day (range: 1 to 9 days) after Dose 1 and 1.0 days (range: 1 to 6 days) after Dose 2.

The onset of most solicited systemic ARs was within the first 2 days after each dose, with a median onset at Day 2 (range: 1 to 7 days) after both doses. Irritability/crying, the most frequently reported solicited systemic AR, had a median onset at Day 2 (range: 1 to 6 days) after Dose 1 and at Day 2 (range: 1 to 7 days) after Dose 2. Solicited systemic ARs had a median duration of 1.0 days (range: 1 to 9 days) after Dose 1 and 1.0 day (range: 1 to 72 days) after Dose 2.

Solicited local ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the first study vaccination occurred in 1/399 participants (0.3%) (pain, Grade 1, up to Day 9); there were no solicited local ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the second study vaccination.

Solicited systemic ARs persisting beyond 7 days after the first study vaccination occurred in 4/399 participants (1.0%), including fever in 2 participants, irritability/crying and loss of appetite in 1 participant, and irritability/crying, sleepiness, and loss of appetite in 1 participant. One of the events of irritability/crying was Grade 3, while the remaining events were Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity. Persistent solicited systemic ARs after the second study vaccination occurred in 4/386 participants (1.0%), including loss of appetite in 3 participants, and sleepiness and loss of appetite in 1 participant. All 4 events of loss of appetite were Grade 3, while sleepiness was Grade 1.

## Medication Use for Solicited Adverse Reactions of Pain and Fever

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Medications taken to treat or prevent pain or fever after dosing were used by 34/199 participants (17.1%), with 25/199 participants (12.6%) using them to treat pain or fever and 14/199 participants (7.0%) using them to prevent pain or fever.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months Through &lt;2 Years)

Medications taken to treat or prevent pain or fever after Dose 1 and Dose 2 were used by 87/399 participants (21.8%) and 88/386 participants (22.8%), respectively. Overall, medications were more frequently used by participants to treat rather than prevent pain or fever after Dose 1 and Dose 2 (60/399 [15.0%] and 67/386 [17.4%] versus 37/399 [9.3%] and 32/386 [8.3%], respectively).

## Fevers Reported During the 7 Days After Any Dose

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

Fever within 7 days after the single dose was reported for 17/199 participants (8.5%). Fever in the range of ≥38°C to &lt;39°C was reported for 11/199 participants (5.5%), while 6/199 participants (3.0%) had fevers ranging from ≥39°C to ≤40°C. No participants experienced a fever of &gt;40°C.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months Through &lt;2 Years)

Fever within 7 days after any dose was reported for 71/399 participants (17.8%). Fever in the range of ≥ 38°C to &lt;39°C after any dose was reported for 56/399 participants (14.0%), while 15/399 participants (3.8%) had fevers ranging from ≥39°C to ≤40°C. No participants experienced a fever of &gt;40°C.

## Unsolicited Adverse Events

Most unsolicited AEs, MAAEs and SAEs across the study consisted of respiratory-related infections, associated symptoms, and other illnesses typical for this childhood population. There were no deaths, AEs leading to discontinuation of study vaccine and/or study participation, cases of MIS-C, myocarditis and/or pericarditis, or anaphylaxis related to study vaccine in any of the study parts. Overall, with up to 12 months safety follow-up in Part 1, unsolicited AEs were consistent with the known safety profile of mRNA1273, and no new safety concerns were identified.

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

## Overview of Part 1 Unsolicited Adverse Events

During the 28 days after any dose, 138/391 participants (35.3%) reported unsolicited AEs and 112/391 participants (28.6%) reported MAAEs. Severe unsolicited AEs were reported for 2/391 participants (0.5%). Unsolicited AEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator were reported for 4/391 participants (1.0%). Three participants (0.8%) reported SAEs; all were assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. There were no deaths, no unsolicited AEs leading to discontinuation of study vaccine and/or study participation, and no cases of MIS-C, myocarditis, pericarditis, or anaphylaxis reported during the 28 days after any dose. One participant reported an AESI of HenochSchönlein purpura, 3 days after Dose 2, which was assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator.

Beyond 28 days after any study vaccination, SAEs were reported in 7 additional participants (total of 10/391 participants [2.6%] throughout the study in Part 1) and severe unsolicited AEs were reported in 4 additional participants (total of 6/391 participants [1.5%] throughout the study). MAAEs were reported in 242/391 participants (61.9%) throughout the study; none of the MAAEs reported beyond 28 days after any study vaccination were considered related to study vaccine. An additional 3 participants experienced

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by System Organ Class and Preferred Term

Overall, the most frequently reported (≥5.0% of participants) unsolicited AEs during the 28 days after any dose of mRNA-1273.214 were in the SOCs of Infections and infestations (94/391 participants [24.0%]) and Respiratory, thoracic, and mediastinal disorders (25/391 participants [6.4%]). The most frequently reported (≥2.0% of participants) unsolicited AEs by PT were upper respiratory tract infection (27/391 participants [6.9%]), otitis media (22/391 participants [5.6%]), viral upper respiratory tract infection (13/391 participants [3.3%]), and rhinorrhea (8/391 participants [2.0%]). The frequently reported unsolicited AEs were generally representative of illnesses and conditions typical of this childhood population.

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by Maximum Intensity

Most unsolicited AEs during the 28 days after any dose were either mild or moderate in severity. A total of 4 severe unsolicited AEs were reported in 2/391 participants (0.5%). One participant experienced 3 events, which included anemia, left ventricular hypertrophy, and cardiac murmur. The other participant experienced severe asthma exacerbation. All of the reported severe events were also SAEs and were assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. Brief summaries of the severe unsolicited AEs are as follows:

- Anemia, left ventricular hypertrophy, and cardiac murmur: A 2-year-old participant experienced severe anemia relative Day 7 after Dose 2, and left ventricular hypertrophy and cardiac murmur on relative day 9 after Dose 2. The participant presented to the emergency department with 2 episodes of syncope. Evaluation revealed hemoglobin level of 2.6 (units not provided) associated with a dietary defect (very limited food intake due to underlying food aversion, for which evaluation with speech therapy was already underway) and all cardiac symptoms were considered related to underlying anemia. Treatment included packed red blood cell transfusions, furosemide, and oral iron supplements. The events were considered resolving at the end of Part 1.
- Asthma: A 5-year-old participant experienced severe asthma (verbatim term: asthma exacerbation) on relative day 14 after Dose 1, which was considered resolved on Relative Day 36. The participant had a known history of asthma, and the exacerbation was associated with symptoms of runny nose and wheezing. The participant required admission to the intensive care unit but was discharged from hospital in 2 days (on relative day 16).

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by Relationship

Unsolicited AEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator during the 28 days after any dose were reported for 4/391 participants (1.0%), including diarrhoea (2/391 participants [0.5%]), and croup infectious and Henoch-Schönlein purpura (1/391 participants [0.3%] each). None of these events were considered serious or severe.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

## Overview of Part 2 Unsolicited Adverse Events

In Part 2, during the 28 days after the BD, 112/539 participants (20.8%) reported unsolicited AEs; 10/539 participants (1.9%) had unsolicited AEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. MAAEs during this period were reported for 75/539 participants (13.9%); 3/539 participants (0.6%) had MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. There were no deaths, no severe unsolicited AEs, no SAEs, no unsolicited AEs leading to study participation discontinuation, and no cases of MIS-C, myocarditis, pericarditis, or anaphylaxis reported during the 28 days after the BD. One participant (0.2%) experienced an AESI (erythema multiforme), which was assessed as related to mRNA1273.214 by the Investigator.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Throughout Part 2, 310/539 participants (57.5%) reported MAAEs. There were no additional MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator besides those reported during the 28 days after the BD. Severe unsolicited AEs and SAEs were reported for 6/539 participants (1.1%) and 9/539 participants (1.7%), respectively. AESIs were reported for 4/539 participants (0.7%); the only AESI assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator was the event of erythema multiforme, which occurred during the 28 days after the BD. There were no deaths, no unsolicited AEs leading to study participation discontinuation, and no cases of MIS-C, myocarditis, or pericarditis were reported throughout Part 2.

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by System Organ Class and Preferred Term

The most frequently reported unsolicited AEs during the 28 days after the BD were in the SOC of Infections and infestations (82/539 participants [15.2%]) followed by Respiratory, thoracic, and mediastinal disorders (11/539 participants [2.0%]). The most frequently reported (≥1.0% of participants) unsolicited AEs by PT were upper respiratory tract infection (31/539 [5.8%]), otitis media (10/539 [1.9%]), pyrexia (7/539 [1.3%]), and rhinorrhea (6/539 [1.1%]).

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

## Overview of Part 3 Unsolicited Adverse Events

During 28 days after study vaccination, at least 1 unsolicited AE was reported in 49/274 participants (17.9%), of which 23/274 (8.4%) were MAAEs. SAEs were reported in 3/274 participants (1.1%), severe unsolicited AEs in 2/274 participants (0.7%), and AESIs in 2/274 participants (0.7%). Unsolicited AEs assessed as related to study vaccination by the Investigator were reported in 3/274 participants (1.1%); all related unsolicited AEs were non serious, and none were severe. There were no deaths and no unsolicited AEs leading to discontinuation from study participation reported.

Throughout the study, 66/274 participants (24.1%) reported MAAEs. SAEs were reported in 5/274 participants (1.8%), severe unsolicited AEs in 3/274 participants (1.1%), and AESIs in 2/274 participants (0.7%). There were no deaths, no unsolicited AEs leading to discontinuation from the study participation, and no additional AEs assessed as related to study vaccine reported beyond 28 days after study vaccination.

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by System Organ Class and Preferred Term

Within 28 days after study vaccination, a total of 68 unsolicited AEs were reported in 49/274 participants (17.9%). Frequently reported unsolicited AEs were generally representative of common childhood illnesses such as respiratory-related or other infections. Unsolicited AEs occurring in ≥1% of participants included nasopharyngitis (15/274 participants [5.5%]), upper respiratory tract infection (4/274 participants [1.5%]), gastroenteritis, and rhinorrhea (3/274 participants [1.1%] each).

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by Maximum Intensity

Most unsolicited AEs during 28 days after study vaccination were either mild or moderate in severity, with 3 severe AEs reported in 2/274 participants (0.7%) including LRTI and UTI in 1 participant and status epilepticus in 1 participant. These were medically attended and serious, were not considered related to study vaccination by the Investigator, and all resolved.

## Unsolicited Adverse Events by Relationship

During the 28 days after study vaccination, 4 unsolicited AEs considered related to study vaccination by the Investigator were reported in 3/274 participants (1.1%), including diarrhoea and vomiting in 1 participant, and gastroenteritis and rash papular in 1 participant each. None of these events were medically attended, severe, or serious, and all resolved.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Overview of Part 4 Unsolicited Adverse Events

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

During the 28 days after the single dose, 39/199 participants (19.6%) reported unsolicited AEs. MAAEs were reported in 19/199 participants (9.5%). There were no deaths, no SAEs, no severe unsolicited AEs, no unsolicited AEs leading to study participation discontinuation, and no AESIs, including no cases of MISC, myocarditis, pericarditis, or anaphylaxis. There were no unsolicited AEs assessed as related to mRNA1273.815 by the Investigator.

Throughout the study, MAAEs were reported in 31/199 participants (15.6%). There were no deaths, no SAEs, no severe unsolicited AEs, no unsolicited AEs leading to study participation discontinuation, and no AESIs, including no cases of MIS-C, myocarditis, pericarditis, or anaphylaxis. There were no unsolicited AEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.815 by the Investigator.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 Months Through &lt;2 Years)

During 28 days after any study vaccination, at least 1 unsolicited AE was reported in 207/399 participants (51.9%) of which 145/399 participants (36.3%) reported MAAEs. SAEs were reported by 10/399 participants (2.5%), severe unsolicited AEs by 3/399 participants (0.8%), and 1 participant (0.3%) reported an AESI of seizure (also reported as an SAE).

Throughout the study, 172/399 participants (43.1%) reported MAAEs. An additional 3 participants reported SAEs beyond 28 days after any study vaccination (total of 13/399 participants [3.3%]) and 1 additional participant reported a severe unsolicited AE (total of 4/399 participants [1.0%]). No additional AESIs were reported beyond 28 days.

There were no deaths, no unsolicited AEs leading to discontinuation of study vaccine and/or study participation, and no cases of MIS-C, myocarditis, pericarditis, or anaphylaxis reported throughout the study.

## Deaths, Serious Adverse Events, and Other Significant Adverse Events

## Deaths

No deaths were reported in Parts 1, 2, 3, or 4 of Study P306.

## Other Serious Adverse Events

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

During the 28 days after any dose, 3/391 participants (0.8%) reported 6 SAEs and the events were bronchiolitis and pneumonia (in a single participant); anemia, left ventricular hypertrophy, and cardiac murmur (in a single participant); and asthma exacerbation.

In addition to these 6 SAEs, an additional 13 SAEs were reported beyond the 28-day postvaccination period throughout Part 1. The additional SAEs included respiratory syncytial virus infection (2 participants); dehydration and anemia (2 events of each in the same participant), vomiting, and hypoglycemia (in a single participant); and staphylococcal scalded skin syndrome, anaphylactic reaction, hypoacusis, asthma, and aberrant aortic arch (1 participant each). The SAE of asthma was reported in the same participant with a previously reported SAE of asthma exacerbation during the 28-day postvaccination period. All SAEs were assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator and were considered either resolved or resolving at the end of Part 1.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

No SAEs were reported during the 28 days after the BD. Throughout Part 2, 9/539 participants (1.7%) reported 10 SAEs, including respiratory syncytial virus bronchiolitis (2/539 participants [0.4%]); pneumonia and otitis media (in a single participant); and dehydration, parainfluenzae virus infection, respiratory syncytial virus infection, febrile convulsion, cerebellar ataxia, and adenoidal hypertrophy (1 participant each). All SAEs were considered resolved and were assessed as not related to mRNA1273.214 by the Investigator.

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

There were 4 SAEs reported in 3/274 participants (1.1%) during 28 days after study vaccination including respiratory tract infection and status epilepticus in one participant each and lower respiratory tract infection and urinary tract infection in the third participant. Throughout the study, 7 SAEs were reported in 5/274 participants (1.8%). The additional events occurring beyond 28 days after study vaccination were febrile convulsion (in the same participant who previously experienced the SAE of status epilepticus), tooth infection and pneumonia. The participant who experienced the SAEs of status

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

epilepticus (onset Study Day 5) and febrile convulsion (onset Study Day 43) had a medical history of glioblastoma and epilepsy. None of the SAEs were considered related to study vaccination by the Investigator.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

No SAEs were reported in Cohort A (Part 4A).

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 months Through &lt;2 Years)

During the 28 days after any dose, 10/399 participants (2.5%) reported 12 SAEs, including pneumonia and pneumonia respiratory syncytial viral (3/399 participants [0.8%] each); bronchiolitis (2/399 participants [0.5%]); and bronchitis, seizure, gastroenteritis clostridial, and herpangina (1/399 participants [0.3%] each). Among these, the SAEs of seizure, gastroenteritis clostridial, 2 pneumonia events, and 1 pneumonia respiratory syncytial viral event were severe. Seizure was also reported as an AESI.

Beyond the 28-day postvaccination period, 3 participants experienced 4 additional SAEs (a total of 16 SAEs in 13/399 participants [3.3%] throughout), including bronchial obstruction and pneumonia (in a single participant) and pneumonia and bronchiolitis (1/399 participant [0.3%] each). One of the pneumonia events was severe. All SAEs reported in Cohort B were assessed as not related to mRNA1273.815 by the Investigator and were considered resolved.

Eleven of the 13 participants with SAEs were from Panama and the events occurred during a period of increased reporting rates of respiratory-related viral infections in that country and region (PAHO 2025). Review of the cases revealed a spectrum of lower respiratory involvement from milder cases (no oxygen requirement) to 2 participants who were placed on mechanical ventilatory support. Treatment included bronchodilators, corticosteroids, and antibiotics in most cases, and the cases depicted clinical presentations and resolutions typical for lower respiratory tract infections in infants and toddlers. No safety concerns were identified upon review of the cases.

## Adverse Events Leading to Discontinuation

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

There were no discontinuations of study vaccine or study participation due to unsolicited AEs reported up to 28 days after any study vaccination or throughout the study in Part 1.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

There were no study discontinuations due to unsolicited AEs reported up to 28 days after study vaccination of the BD or throughout the study in Part 2.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Medically Attended Adverse Events

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

During the 28 days after any dose, MAAEs were reported for 112/391 participants (28.6%), and events were mainly from the Infections and infestations SOC (22.5%). Upper respiratory tract infection (6.4%), otitis media (5.6%), and viral upper respiratory tract infection (2.8%) were the only unsolicited MAAEs reported in &gt;2.0% of participants.

MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.21 by the Investigator during the 28 days after any dose were reported for 2 participants (0.5%), including diarrhoea and Henoch-Schönlein purpura (1 participant [0.3%] each). No additional MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator were reported beyond the 28-day postvaccination period.

Throughout Part 1, MAAEs were reported for 242/391 participants (61.9%). The type and nature of events were similar to those reported during the 28 days after any dose. The majority (53.5%) of events were in the Infections and infestations SOC and overall, the events mostly represented acute infections and associated symptoms common in this pediatric population.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Overall, a total of 75/539 participants (13.9%) reported unsolicited MAAEs during the 28 days after the BD. Most of these events (12.2%) were in the Infections and infestations SOC and were representative of typical illnesses for this pediatric population. Upper respiratory tract infection (5.0%) and otitis media (1.9%) were the only unsolicited MAAEs reported in &gt;1.0% of participants during the 28 days after the BD.

MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator during the 28 days after the BD were reported for 3 participants (0.6%), including lymphadenopathy (cervical chain and posterior auricular), erythema multiforme, and urticaria (1 participant [0.2%] each). No additional MAAEs assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator were reported beyond the 28-day post-BD period.

Throughout Part 2, MAAEs were reported for 310/539 participants (57.5%). The type and nature of events were similar to those reported during the 28 days after the BD. The majority (52.7%) of events were in the Infections and infestations SOC and overall, the events mostly represented acute infections and associated symptoms common in this pediatric population.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

During 28 days after study vaccination, 28 MAAEs were reported in 23/274 participants (8.4%), 3 of which were severe (in 2 participants [0.7%]). Most of these events (15/274 participants [5.5%]) were in the Infections and infestations SOC and were representative of common illnesses typical for this pediatric population. The only MAAEs reported in more than 1 participant were nasopharyngitis (3/274 participants [1.1%]), and conjunctivitis, gastroenteritis viral, and rhinorrhea (2/274 participants [0.7%] each). One participant experienced a severe MAAE of LRTI and UTI, and 1 participant experienced a severe MAAE of status epilepticus. All severe MAAEs were in the ≥2 years to &lt;6 years age subgroup.

Throughout the study, 97 MAAEs were reported in 66/274 participants (24.1%), 5 of which were severe (in 3 participants [1.1%]). Most of these events (56/274 participants [20.4%]) were in the Infections and infestations SOC, and the most frequently reported events were upper respiratory tract infection (15/274 participants [5.5%]), nasopharyngitis (10/247 participants [3.6%]), and otitis media acute (6/274 participants [2.2%]). All severe MAAEs were in the ≥2 years to &lt;6 years age subgroup.

None of the MAAEs were considered related to study vaccination by the Investigator.

## Part 4: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) and 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;2 Years)

## Cohort A (mRNA-1273.815 Single Dose; 2 Years Through &lt;5 Years)

During 28 days after study vaccination, a total of 19/199 participants (9.5%) reported MAAEs. Most of these events (15/199 participants [7.5%]) were in the Infections and infestations SOC and were representative of common illnesses typical for this pediatric population. Nasopharyngitis (10/199 participants [5.0%]) and diarrhoea (2/199 participants [1.0%]) were the only MAAEs reported in more than 1 participant.

Throughout the study, a total of 31/199 participants (15.6%) reported MAAEs. Most of these events (13.6%) were in the Infections and infestations SOC and the most frequently reported events were nasopharyngitis (16/199 participants [8.0%]), upper respiratory tract infection (4/199 participants [2.0%]), and diarrhoea (3/199 participants [1.5%]). Overall, the events generally represented acute infections and associated symptoms common in this pediatric population.

None of the MAAEs were severe or considered related to study vaccine by the Investigator.

## Cohort B (mRNA-1273.815 2-Dose Series; 6 Months Through &lt;2 Years)

During the 28 days after any dose, MAAEs were reported for 145/399 participants (36.3%). The events were mainly from the Infections and infestations SOC (127/399 participants [31.8%]). Nasopharyngitis (56/399 participants [14.0%]) and respiratory tract infection and gastroenteritis (13/399 participants [3.3%] each) were the only MAAEs reported in &gt;2.0% of participants.

Severe MAAEs were reported for 3/399 participants (0.8%), including pneumonia respiratory syncytial viral and gastroenteritis clostridial (in a single participant); pneumonia and seizure (in a single participant), and pneumonia (1 participant). All severe MAAEs were also reported as SAEs.

Throughout the study, a total of 172/399 participants (43.1%) reported MAAEs after any dose. The type and nature of MAAEs were similar to those reported during the 28-day postvaccination period. Overall, the events generally represented acute infections and associated symptoms common in this pediatric population. None of the MAAEs were considered related to study vaccine by the Investigator.

## Adverse Events of Special Interest (Protocol-defined Adverse Events of Special Interest and Programmed Standardized MedDRA Queries)

## Multisystem Inflammatory Syndrome in Children

No AESIs of MIS-C were reported throughout Parts 1, 2, 3, and 4 of Study P306.

## Protocol-defined Adverse Events of Special Interest

## Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Throughout Part 1, 4/391 participants (1.0%) reported AESIs, and the events were febrile convulsion (3 participants [0.8%]) and Henoch-Schönlein purpura (1/391 participants [0.3%]). Of these, the AESI of Henoch-Schönlein purpura occurred during the 28-day postvaccination period and was assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. A brief summary of the event follows:

- An 11-month-old participant experienced a moderate non-serious AESI of Henoch-Schönlein purpura 3 days after Dose 2 (Relative Day 3) after presenting with rash, primarily on the bilateral lower extremities, with some patches on her hands, feet, shoulders, and face. The lesions were dark red and dark purple with varying sizes containing both raised and flat patches. The participant was voiding and drinking normally. She had no pain, fever, blood in urine, or ongoing solicited reactions. Diagnosis was confirmed by the primary care physician. Urine analysis was normal with +1 leukocytes (reference range not provided); urine culture was performed (results not provided). Complete blood count showed elevated platelet count (491×10 3 /µL [reference range: 140 to 400×10 3 /µL]) and elevated erythrocyte sedimentation rate (31 mm/h [reference range: ≤20 mm/h]). The event was considered resolved on Relative Day 11. The Investigator assessed the event as related to mRNA-1273.214 as no other predisposing factors could be identified other than temporal association with the study vaccine.

No events of febrile convulsion were reported during the 28 days after any dose. Beyond the 28-day postvaccination period throughout Part 1, febrile seizure was reported in 3/391 participants (0.8%). These 3 events occurred in participants aged between 17 months and 2 years, ≥99 days after Dose 2, and all 3 cases reported concurrent respiratory tract infections. All cases were assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator.

## Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years)

Throughout Part 2, AESIs were reported for 4/539 participants (0.7%), including febrile convulsion (2 participants) and erythema multiforme and seizure (1 participant each).

The AESI of erythema multiforme occurred during the 28 days after the BD and was assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator; a brief summary of the event is as follows:

- A 4-year-old participant experienced a mild AESI of mild erythema multiforme (verbatim term: generalized erythema multiforme) 1 day after the BD. The rash was characterized by multiple raised, red patches, some with central pallor and raised borders, bilaterally on arms, legs, feet, and on the back. The lesions were not pruritic and there was no facial or joint swelling. Treatment for the event included oral hydroxyzine and oral cetirizine hydrochloride. The event of erythema

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

multiforme was considered resolved 7 days after the onset of rash. Of note, the participant had recently been treated with silver sulfadiazine for bacterial folliculitis, which is known to be associated with erythema multiforme.

No events of febrile seizure were reported during the 28 days after the BD. Beyond the 28-day period after the BD, febrile seizure was reported for 2/539 participants (0.4%) (of these, 1 event of febrile convulsion was reported as an AESI and an SAE and 1 event was not reported as an AESI), occurring between 121 and 165 days after the BD. None of the events were assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator.

There were 2 AESIs reported in 2/274 participants (0.7%) during the 28 days after study vaccination, transaminases increased and status epilepticus in one participant each and a total of 3 AESIs reported in 2/274 participants (0.7%) throughout the study up (additional event of febrile seizure in the participant who previously experienced the AESI of status epilepticus). None of these AESIs were assessed as related to study vaccination by the Investigator.
