## Additional Analyses and Review of Specific AESIs and SMQs

## Myocarditis and Pericarditis

Summaries of the findings from these evaluations for each part of Study P306 are presented below:

Parts 1 and 2:

No unsolicited AEs of myocarditis or pericarditis were reported. Review of the unsolicited AEs reported within the Cardiomyopathy SMQ and age-appropriate PTs from the CDC working case definitions for acute myocarditis and pericarditis did not identify any events suggestive of either. As such, no cases were sent to the CEAC for adjudication up to study completion of study Parts 1 and 2.

Part 3:

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

No unsolicited AEs of myocarditis or pericarditis were reported and no AESIs were identified upon review of the Cardiomyopathy SMQ and age-appropriate PTs from the CDC case definitions of acute myocarditis and pericarditis.

## Part 4:

No unsolicited AEs of myocarditis or pericarditis were reported in either Cohort A or Cohort B.

Within the Cardiomyopathy SMQ in Cohort B, there was 1 mild unsolicited AE of dyspnea (verbatim term: intermittent gasping, etiology unknown) that occurred in a 1-year-old participant on Study Day 14. The event was assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.815 by the Investigator and was ongoing at the time of the data cutoff. No treatment was documented. The MAH was asked to provide a narrative for this case and follow-up information as there is a temporal relationship between vaccination and onset of symptoms. The MAH provided the narrative that indicated seasonal allergy and treatment with loratadine. The assessment of no relatedness by the Investigator is agreed.

Analysis of age-appropriate PTs reported within 14 days of vaccination and potentially indicative of myocarditis and pericarditis (based on the MedDRA terms v.23.0 included in the CDC working case definitions for acute myocarditis and acute pericarditis [Gargano et al 2021]) did not identify any events suggestive of either. As such, no cases in Cohort A or Cohort B were sent to the cardiac event adjudication committee for adjudication.

## Hypersensitivity

Summaries of the findings from specific evaluations of hypersensitivity are presented below for each part of Study P306:

Part 1: mRNA-1273.214 (25 μg) Primary Series (6 Months Through 5 Years):

Within the narrow and broad Hypersensitivity SMQ, 21/391 participants (5.4%) reported at least 1 unsolicited AE during the 28 days after any dose. The most frequently reported (in &gt;2 participants) unsolicited AEs within the Hypersensitivity SMQ were seasonal allergy (4 participants [1.0%]) and urticaria (3 participants [0.8%]). The only unsolicited AE assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator was Henoch-Schönlein purpura. There was 1 severe unsolicited AE of asthma (also an SAE); all other events were mild or moderate in severity. There were no cases of anaphylaxis reported during the 28 days after any dose.

Beyond the 28-day postvaccination period, 1/391 participants (0.3%) experienced a moderate anaphylactic reaction 142 days after Dose 2 due to an unknown food allergy; the event was an SAE, resolved on the same day of onset, and was assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator.

Part 2: mRNA-1273.214 BD (10 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years):

During the 28 days after the BD, 13/539 participants (2.4%) reported unsolicited AEs within the Hypersensitivity SMQ, with no reports of anaphylaxis or severe events. Two cases of urticaria were assessed as related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator. A 3 year old participant had moderate urticaria 5 days post-BD, which was treated with diphenhydramine and loratadine and resolved in 4 days. A 1-year-old participant developed moderate urticaria 6 days post-BD, with concurrent vomiting and diarrhoea, was treated with loratadine, and the urticaria resolved in 3 days.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Throughout Part 2, unsolicited AEs within the narrow and broad Hypersensitivity SMQ were reported in 39/539 participants (7.2%) (including those that were reported during the 28 days after the BD). Conjunctivitis (12/539 participants [2.2%]), urticaria (8/539 participants [1.5%]), and seasonal allergy (7/539 [1.3%] participants) were the only unsolicited AE within this SMQ that were reported in at least 1.0% of participants.

One participant experienced a moderate nonserious anaphylactic reaction (verbatim term: anaphylaxis caused by cashews) 168 days after the BD. The event resolved on the same day of onset and was assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.214 by the Investigator.

Part 3: mRNA-1273.815 BD (25 μg) (6 Months Through 5 Years):

Within the narrow and broad Hypersensitivity SMQ, 7/274 participants (2.6%) had an event within 28 days after study vaccination and 12/274 participants (4.4%) had an event throughout the study. No events of anaphylaxis or other severe hypersensitivity events were identified, and none of the events reported were considered related to study vaccination by the Investigator.

Part 4A: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) Single Dose (2 to &lt;5 Years) (Cohort A):

Within the narrow and broad Hypersensitivity SMQ, 3/199 participants (1.5%) reported an unsolicited AE during the 28 days after the single dose and 4/199 participants (2.0%) throughout the study. No events of anaphylaxis or other severe hypersensitivity events were identified and none of the events reported were considered related to study vaccine by the Investigator.

Part 4B: mRNA-1273.815 (25 μg) 2 Dose Series (6 Months to &lt;Years) (Cohort B):

Within the narrow and broad Hypersensitivity SMQ, 21/399 participants (5.3%) reported an unsolicited AE during the 28 days after any dose and 29/399 participants (7.3%) throughout the study. No events of anaphylaxis or other severe hypersensitivity-related events were reported.

One case of mild urticaria (verbatim term: urticaria on the face and chest [probably food allergy]) was reported for 1/399 participant (0.3%) 4 days after Dose 1. The event was non-serious, resolved on the same day, and was assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.815 by the Investigator.

## Narratives

Individual participant narratives for SAEs, AESIs, AEs leading to discontinuation, and certain other clinically meaningful AEs are provided in the respective CSRs for each part of Study P306 (Study P306 Final CSR Part 1 and Part 2, Section 15; Study P306 Final CSR Part 3, Section 14.3.3; Study P306 Final CSR Part 4A, Section 15; Study P306 Part 4 CSR Addendum, Section 14.3.3).

## 7.3. Discussion

In Part 1, mRNA-1273.214 25 µg was administered as a 2 dose primary series in participants 6 months through 5 years of age. A total of 378 participants received both doses. Throughout Part 1, the median duration of follow-up was 358.0 days from Dose 2. A total of 360 participants (92.1%) and 327

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

participants (83.6%) had ≥6 months (i.e., ≥168 days) and ≥12 months (i.e., ≥336 days) of safety followup after Dose 2 of mRNA-1273.214, respectively.

Solicited systemic ARs were reported for 44.5% and 43.2% of participants after Dose 1 and Dose 2 of mRNA-1273.214, respectively. One solicited systemic AR of Grade 4 (fever) was reported for 1 participants (0.3%) after Dose 2. The Grade 4 fever (104.1°F/40.1°C) occurred 5 days after Dose 2 in a 5 year old White male participant. The fever was associated with exposure to a sick family member and with concurrent symptoms, and is therefore not considered related to study vaccination.

In Part 2, mRNA-1273.214 10 µg was administered as a BD to 532 participants 6 months through 5 years of age who were previously enrolled in Study P204 and had received mRNA-1273 25 µg 2 dose primary series. The median duration of follow-up after the mRNA-1273.214 BD was 184.0 days. A total of 97.2% of participants had ≥6 months (i.e., ≥168 days) of safety follow-up after the BD.

A percentage of 68.8% of the participants reported at least 1 solicited AR during the 7 days after the BD, with solicited local and systemic ARs reported for 49.9% and 49.2%, respectively.

No Grade 4 (&gt;40.0°C) fevers were reported.

In Part 3, mRNA-1273.815 25 µg was administered as a BD to 274 participants 6 months through 5 years of age. The median study duration after the BD was 181.0 days (range: 11 to 209 days). A total of 97.1% were followed for ≥168 days after BD.

Solicited local ARs were reported by 108/271 participants (39.9%) and solicited systemic ARs were reported by 104/271 participants (38.4%).

There were no Grade 4 fevers.

In Part 4A (Part 4 Cohort A), mRNA-1273.815 25 μg was administered as a single dose to a total of 199 participants 2 to &lt;5 years of age. The median study duration after study vaccination was 92.0 days (range: 64 to 114 days). Participants were followed for approximately 3 months after dosing, with the majority (198/199 participants [99.5%]) followed for ≥84 days from dosing.

Solicited local ARs were reported for 59/199 participants (29.6%) within 7 days after the single dose. Solicited systemic ARs were reported for 47/199 participants (23.6%) within 7 days after the single dose.

No participants experienced a fever of &gt;40°C.

In Part 4B (Part 4 Cohort B), mRNA-1273.815 25 µg was administered as a 2 dose primary series to participants 6 months to &lt;2 years of age. All 399 participants received the first study vaccination, while 386 participants received both doses. The median study duration was 118 days (range: 8 to 191 days) from the first study vaccination and 86 days (range: 0 to 104 days) from the second study vaccination. Most participants (238/399 [59.6%]) were followed for ≥84 days after the second study vaccination.

The incidence of solicited local ARs as well as solicited systemic ARs was similar for participants in part 4 Cohort B after Dose 1 and Dose 2.

No participants experienced a fever of &gt;40°C.

Overall, numbers of participants and safety follow-up time is considered acceptable for the purpose of the submission.

Across all study parts, most unsolicited AEs, MAAEs and SAEs consisted of respiratory-related infections, associated symptoms, and other illnesses typical for the childhood population. There were no deaths, AEs leading to discontinuation of study vaccine and/or study participation, cases of MIS-C, myocarditis and/or pericarditis, or anaphylaxis related to study vaccine in any of the study parts.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Overall, solicited and unsolicited AEs were consistent with the known safety profile of mRNA-1273, and no new safety concerns were identified.

## 8. Risk management plan

## 8.1. 1 st round

The MAH submitted an updated RMP version 14.0, dated 03 Mar 2026, with this application. The (main) proposed RMP changes were the following:

- To remove Spikevax, Spikevax bivalent Original/Omicron BA.1, Spikevax bivalent Original/Omicron BA.4-5, and Spikevax XBB.1.5 in line with the deletion of strains approved during procedure EMEA/VR/0000316350.
- To remove long-term safety as missing information following the completion of Study mRNA 1273-P306 and based on the extensive safety information for Spikevax and its variant-containing vaccines.
- To remove completed Study mRNA-1273-P306 as an additional PV activity.
- To update the paediatric posology for Spikevax JN.1 and Spikevax LP.8.1.

## Safety Specification

## Identified and potential risks

## SVII.2 New Safety Concerns and Reclassification With a Submission of an Updated RMP

Long-term safety previously classified as missing information is removed from the list of safety concerns Long-term safety was included as missing information in the RMP at the time of initial conditional marketing authorisation for Spikevax in the EU (06 Jan 2021).

Changes in the level of scientific evidence for the causal association or risk-benefit impact:

The long-term safety profile of Spikevax and its variant-containing formulations has been extensively characterised across a large, mature evidence base spanning clinical trials, post marketing observational research, and population-level health record analyses.

Study mRNA-1273-P306 fulfils the final long-term safety commitment.

Clinical development evidence: Moderna's clinical development programme for Spikevax and its variantcontaining formulations has included multiple clinical trials evaluating safety, including studies sponsored by ModernaTx, Inc. Cumulatively, more than 65,000 participants have been exposed to mRNA-1273 or its variant-containing formulations, including participants who received mRNA-1273 (or variants) concomitantly with other Moderna investigational vaccines (mRNA-1283, mRNA-1010, mRNA-1345) or with active, licensed seasonal influenza vaccines within the ModernaTx, Inc.-sponsored mRNA clinical development programme. Studies assessing long-term safety (protocol-specified safety follow-up of ≥12 months) include Study mRNA-1273-P306 and the completed studies Study mRNA-1273-P301, Study 200003, Study mRNA-1273-P203, Study mRNA-1273-P204, and Study mRNA-1273-P205.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Post-authorisation observational evidence: Moderna also conducted 2 observational studies as additional PV activities targeting characterisation of long-term safety for Spikevax and its variant-containing formulations. Study mRNA-1273-P903 was a retrospective observational cohort study that used secondary, de identified individual-level medical and pharmacy claims data provided by HealthVerity. Components of active vaccine surveillance via a historically controlled comparator and signal refinement using a selfcontrolled risk interval design were implemented, and a final study report was submitted on 30 Jun 2023. The study included 6,824,063 adults and 9,046 children receiving at ≥1 dose of Spikevax. Results were largely consistent with the known safety profile for Spikevax and its variant-containing vaccines, particularly for labelled events such as myocarditis and anaphylaxis. mRNA-1273-P904 was an observational post-marketing multi-database retrospective study that utilised routinely collected secondary data from population registries in Denmark and Norway, as well as primary care-based databases in Spain and the UK. The study was designed to evaluate the safety of both the original Spikevax and the bivalent Spikevax in the EU by assessing whether the occurrence of each AESI among individuals vaccinated with Spikevax and variant-containing formulations in Europe was higher than the expected occurrence of that AESI in the same population in the absence of Spikevax. The final study report was submitted on 31 Mar 2025. The study included 564,080 individuals in Denmark, 531,172 in Norway, 621,871 in Spain, and 273,254 in the UK who received ≥1 dose of Spikevax. No new safety concerns were identified beyond those previously reported for the original Spikevax. Although data were limited for bivalent Spikevax, no concerning safety patterns were observed. Population-level survival evidence: A recently published national cohort study including the entire adult population aged 18 to 59 years in France (approximately 28 million persons) used a national health records system to evaluate COVID-19 vaccination outcomes. mRNA COVID-19 vaccination was associated with a 74% reduction in risk of death from severe COVID-19 and a 25% lower risk of all-cause mortality, with no increase in deaths from major conditions such as cardiovascular disease or cancer (Semenzato et al 2025). Collectively, these data support that updated mRNA COVID-19 vaccines provide effective protection against contemporary SARS CoV-2 variants, supported by extensive post-authorisation safety monitoring and without evidence of increased long-term mortality risk. Scale of real-world exposure and ongoing monitoring: The extensive use of Spikevax and its variantcontaining vaccines (estimated &gt;1 billion individuals vaccinated with ≥1 dose), has provided extensive

safety information for use of the vaccines in the general population.

## Conclusion

Based on the totality of evidence now available, including (i) a large clinical development programme with ≥12 months protocol-specified safety follow-up in multiple studies, (ii) large-scale post-marketing observational evidence in Europe (mRNA-1273-P904) showing no new safety concerns, and (iii) population-level national health record evidence demonstrating reduced all-cause mortality and no increase in cause-specific mortality for major conditions (Semenzato et al 2025), Moderna considers that \"long-term safety\" no longer meets the criteria for missing information as the long-term safety profile of Spikevax and its variant-containing vaccines have been well characterised. Ongoing PV activities (routine surveillance and scheduled signal detection) will continue to monitor for any potential late-emerging risks, and any new information will be evaluated and reported in accordance with applicable regulatory body requirements.

The important identified risks \"myocarditis\" and \"pericarditis\", were consequentially updated to include information from completed Study mRNA-1273-P306 relating to myocarditis and pericarditis.

## PRAC Rapporteur's Comment

The MAH proposed to remove long-term safety as missing information following the completion of Study mRNA 1273-P306, which fulfils the long-term safety commitment, and additionally based on the extensive safety information for Spikevax and its variant-containing vaccines. It is agreed that long term safety can no longer be rightfully categorised as missing information in light of the vast amount of cumulative information from the clinical development programme as well as real-world exposure data available. Following completion of the Study mRNA 1273-P306 as the last commitment tied to the longterm safety category, the removal of long-term safety is acceptable.

## Clinical aspects

Efficacy:

1. What has not been demonstrated with the submitted results is the comparison in terms of GMTs of a single booster dose in subjects 2 - 4 years of age that have not been vaccinated previously and no history of SARS-CoV-2 infection versus 2-dose primary series vaccination. The MAH argues that sero-epidemiology has changed, and subjects in this age group can be considered seropositive for SARS-CoV-2. This needs to be further substantiated, and/or non-inferiority in terms of GMTs of a single dose in 2 - 4-year-old SARS-CoV-2 naïve children without prior SARSCoV-2 vaccination versus a 2-dose primary series should be demonstrated.

## 10. Assessment of the responses to the request for supplementary information

## 10.1. Major objections

## Clinical aspects

## Question 1

## Efficacy:

What has not been demonstrated with the submitted results is the comparison in terms of GMTs of a single booster dose in subjects 2 - 4 years of age that have not been vaccinated previously and no

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

history of SARS-CoV-2 infection versus 2-dose primary series vaccination. The MAH argues that seroepidemiology has changed, and subjects in this age group can be considered seropositive for SARS-CoV2. This needs to be further substantiated, and/or non-inferiority in terms of GMTs of a single dose in 2 - 4-year-old SARS-CoV-2 naïve children without prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination versus a 2-dose primary series should be demonstrated.

## Summary of the MAH's response

The MAH acknowledges the Agency's comment. The MAH will no longer pursue the proposed single-dose posology for children aged 2 to &lt;5 years and proposes to maintain the currently approved 2-dose primary series for all children in this age group.

The MAH agrees that the submitted data do not demonstrate non-inferiority of a single dose compared with a 2-dose primary series in SARS-CoV-2-naïve children aged 2 to &lt;5 years. The prespecified noninferiority comparison in Study mRNA-1273-P306 Part 4 evaluated a different comparison: baseline SARS-CoV-2-positive participants aged 2 through 4 years who received a single dose were compared with baseline SARS-CoV-2-negative participants aged 6 through 23 months who received a 2-dose series. Although the prespecified noninferiority criterion based on the GMR of GMCs was met for this comparison, the analysis did not provide a comparison of a single dose versus the 2-dose primary series within SARSCoV-2-naive participants aged 2 through 4 years.

## Assessment of the MAH's response

The MAH does not further pursue a change in the posology to the age group 2 through 4 years and no changes will be made to the SmPC. This is accepted and the issue is resolved.

## 10.2. Other concerns

## Clinical aspects

## Question 2

Safety:

Within the Cardiomyopathy SMQ in Part 4 Cohort B, there was 1 mild unsolicited AE of dyspnea (verbatim term: intermittent gasping, etiology unknown) that occurred in a 1-year-old on Study Day 14. The event was assessed as not related to mRNA-1273.815 by the Investigator and was ongoing at the time of the data cutoff. No treatment was documented. The MAH is asked to provide a narrative for this case and follow-up information as there is a temporal relationship between vaccination and onset of symptoms.

## Summary of the MAH's response

The MAH provided the narrative.
